\end{figure}

\begin{definition}[Graded vertex quadrisection operator]
    \label{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$ with regions $\M_\l$, let $\markedVertexClasses \subseteq \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])$ be a set of vertex classes of $\domseq[\L]$ marked for refinement, and let $\targetlevel$ assign a target level $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \coloneq \targetlevel(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \in \{ \lzero, \, \dots, \, \L \}$ to each class. The \emph{graded vertex quadrisection operator}
    \begin{equation}
        \quadriV \; \colon \; \alldomseqs_\L \times \powerset(\vertexClasses) \times \{ \lzero, \, \dots, \, \L \}^{\vertexClasses} \; \longrightarrow \; \alldomseqs_\L
    \end{equation}
    is defined levelwise by
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:graded_vertex_quadrisection}
        \quadriV \big( \domseq[\L], \, \markedVertexClasses, \, \targetlevel \big) \; \coloneq \; \M_\lzero \, \sqcup \bigsqcup\limits_{\l = \lzero}^{\L-1} \Big( \M_{\l+1} \; \cup \bigcup\limits_{\substack{\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \, \in \, \markedVertexClasses \\ \l \, < \, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone}} \Aop{}{\l}{\l+1} \big( \FVanc{\l}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \big) \Big),
    \end{equation}
    indexed, like the face quadrisection~\eqref{eq:def_face_quadrisection_operator}, by the level $\l$ of the faces that are refined. The condition $\l < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ selects which marked classes contribute on the level $\l$ that the outer union has fixed. Only the restriction of $\targetlevel$ to $\markedVertexClasses$ is ever read, which is what keeps the third factor of the signature independent of the second.
\end{definition}

Figure~\ref{fig:refine_interface} displays the refinement the graded quadrisection produces at the leaf stars of two interface vertices. The target $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone = \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone} \! + \! 1$ used there brings the whole neighbourhood up to the finest level the leaf star already carries and then one level past it.

The vertex quadrisection operator is not a new construction. It is the face quadrisection applied to the family of class stars, and everything that has to be checked about it is the check already made in Definition~\ref{def:face_quadrisection_operator}.

\begin{corollary}[The graded vertex quadrisection is a face quadrisection]
    \label{cor:graded_is_face_quadrisection}
    Let $\domseq[\L]$, $\markedVertexClasses$ and $\targetlevel$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} and put
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:graded_marked_family}
        \simplexsubsetTwo{\l} \; \coloneq \bigcup\limits_{\substack{\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \, \in \, \markedVertexClasses \\ \l \, < \, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone}} \FVanc{\l} \big( \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \big), \qquad \lzero \leq \l \leq \L \! - \! 1.
    \end{equation}
    Then $\simplexsubsetTwo{} = \bigsqcup_\l \simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$ is a marked family for $\domseq[\L]$ in the sense of~\eqref{eq:marked_face_family}, and
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:graded_is_face_quadrisection}
        \quadriV \big( \domseq[\L], \, \markedVertexClasses, \, \targetlevel \big) \; = \; \quadriF \big( \domseq[\L], \, \simplexsubsetTwo{} \big).
    \end{equation}
    In particular the right-hand side of~\eqref{eq:graded_vertex_quadrisection} is again a domain sequence.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
    The two closed forms~\eqref{eq:def_face_quadrisection_operator} and~\eqref{eq:graded_vertex_quadrisection} adjoin the same set to $\M_{\l+1}$ on every level, because $\Aop{}{\l}{\l+1}$ distributes over the union in~\eqref{eq:graded_marked_family}. That the family is a marked one needs no argument on level $\lzero$. For $\l > \lzero$, every class of the index set of level $\l$ is in the index set of level $\l \! - \! 1$ as well, since $\l < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ implies $\l \! - \! 1 < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$, and the nestedness~\eqref{eq:star_ancestry_nested} of the class star gives $\FVanc{\l}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \subseteq \Aop{}{\l-1}{\l}(\FVanc{\l-1}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}))$. Taking the union over the index set yields $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l} \subseteq \Aop{}{\l-1}{\l}(\simplexsubsetTwo{\l-1})$, which is the recursion in~\eqref{eq:marked_face_family} without needing the region at all.
\end{proof}

Our implementation evaluates the graded vertex quadrisection operator through the following lemma, by iterating over the vertex classes and adding their class stars level by level.

\begin{corollary}[Vertexwise decomposition of the graded quadrisection]
    \label{cor:graded_quadrisection_composition}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$, let $\markedVertexClasses$ and $\targetlevel$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection}, and let $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone_1}, \, \dots, \, \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone_{\vert \markedVertexClasses \vert}}$ be any enumeration of $\markedVertexClasses$. Then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:graded_quadrisection_composition}
        \quadriV \big( \domseq[\L], \, \markedVertexClasses, \, \targetlevel \big) \; = \; \domseq[\L]^{\vert \markedVertexClasses \vert}, \qquad \text{with} \quad \domseq[\L]^{p} \coloneq \quadriV \big( \domseq[\L]^{p-1}, \, \{ \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone_p} \}, \, \targetlevel \big) \quad \text{and} \quad  \domseq[\L]^{0} \coloneq \domseq[\L].
    \end{equation}
    In particular the result is independent of the enumeration and a second application of the operator is the identity. Moreover, $\quadriV(\domseq[\L], \markedVertexClasses, \targetlevel) = \domseq[\L]$ is the identity if and only if every $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \markedVertexClasses$ satisfies $\lmin(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \geq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ in $\domseq[\L]$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
    Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} adjoins to each region the union of sets that depend on the class and its target level alone, so both sides of~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_composition} adjoin the same union to $\M_\lone$, and a union does not depend on the order of its terms or on repetitions among them. The last assertion compares two thresholds on the same axis. Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} adjoins something for $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ exactly on the levels $\lone < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$, and~\eqref{eq:class_star_children} says that what it would adjoin is already there exactly on the levels $\lone < \lmin$. If $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \leq \lmin$ for every marked class, the first range is contained in the second and every adjoined set is redundant. If $\lmin < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ for some class, then $\lone = \lmin$ lies in the first range but not in the second, so the operator adjoins a child of $\FVanc{\lmin}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})$ that the second case of~\eqref{eq:class_star_levels} places outside $\M_{\lmin+1}$.
\end{proof}

The operator also fixes what the leaf star of the vertex looks like afterwards, which is what the grading step of Section~\ref{subsec:padding} reads off it.

\begin{corollary}[Leaf star of the output]
    \label{cor:graded_quadrisection_leafstar}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$, let the vertex class $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])$ have leaf star of coarsest level $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ and finest level $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$, and let $\targetlevel$ be a target level map as in Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} with value $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ at $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$. A primed quantity $(\cdot)'$ is read on the output domain sequence throughout.
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=3pt, itemsep=-2pt]
        \item[$(i)$] \emph{The class alone.} On $\domseq[\L]' = \quadriV(\domseq[\L], \{ \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \}, \targetlevel)$ the operator carries both ends to the target level and no further,
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar}
            \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \; = \; \max \big( \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \big) \qquad \text{and} \qquad \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \; = \; \max \big( \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \big) .
        \end{equation}
        The level span is $\levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}' = \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' - \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone} - \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ whenever $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone} < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \leq \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$.

        \item[$(ii)$] \emph{The class inside a marked set.} On $\domseq[\L]' = \quadriV(\domseq[\L], \markedVertexClasses, \targetlevel)$ for a marked set $\markedVertexClasses$ that contains $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$, the two equalities of $(i)$ become the bounds
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar_set}
            \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \; \geq \; \max \big( \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \big)
            \quad \text{and} \quad
            \max \big( \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \big) \; \leq \; \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \; \leq \; \max \big( \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone} \! + \! 1, \, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \big) .
        \end{equation}
        The other classes of $\markedVertexClasses$ may refine into the leaf star of $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ as well. By Lemma~\ref{lemma:meeting_level_leafstar} the other marked classes part from $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ early enough to lift $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}'$ by at most one.

        \item[$(iii)$] \emph{A class outside the marked set.} On the output of $(ii)$, a class $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L]) \setminus \markedVertexClasses$ satisfies $(ii)$ with the target level set to $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxtwo = \lzero$, i.e.
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar_unmarked}
            \lminIdx{\coarseIdxtwo}' \; \geq \; \lminIdx{\coarseIdxtwo}
            \qquad \text{and} \qquad
            \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxtwo} \; \leq \; \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxtwo}' \; \leq \; \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxtwo} \! + \! 1.
        \end{equation}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
    Let $\M'$ denote the regions of the output domain sequence $\domseq[\L]'$.

    \emph{Clause $(i)$.} If $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone} \geq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$, the operator is the identity by Corollary~\ref{cor:graded_quadrisection_composition} and both maxima of~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar} read off their first argument. So let $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone} < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$.

    Write $\FVanc{\lone}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \cap \ancestorPatch{\M'_{\lone+1}}$ for the part of the class star that is refined beyond its own level, read on $\domseq[\L]'$. This is the intersection of~\eqref{eq:class_star_levels}, and Proposition~\ref{proposition:class_star_levels} makes $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}'$ the coarsest level at which it is a proper subset of $\FVanc{\lone}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})$. Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} puts $\Aop{}{\lone}{\lone+1}(\FVanc{\lone}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}))$ into $\M'_{\lone+1}$ for every $\lone < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$. Since $\M'_{\lone+1}$ is quadrisection-closed, the refined part is the whole class star on all those levels, and hence $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \geq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$. The operator adjoins no face on a level above $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$, so $\M'_{\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone+1} = \M_{\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone+1}$ and the refined part at level $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ is $\FVanc{\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \cap \ancestorPatch{\M_{\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone+1}}$. It is a proper subset of $\FVanc{\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})$ by the second or the third case of~\eqref{eq:class_star_levels}, because $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone} \leq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$. Thus $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone = \max(\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone)$.

    For the finest level, the operator refines only faces of the class star on levels $\lone < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$, so a leaf face of the input leaf star on a level $\geq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ is untouched and remains one of the output. If $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \leq \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$, the level-$\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ leaf face survives and nothing finer is added, giving $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$. If $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone > \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$, then $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ by the previous paragraph, and the regions above level $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ are those of the input, which carry no leaf face of this star above $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$, so the output leaf star sits entirely on level $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ and $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$. Both cases are $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \max(\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone)$.

    \emph{Clause $(ii)$.} Adding the remaining marked classes only grows the regions, so a face refined in the output domain sequence of $(i)$ is refined here as well. Below $\max(\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone)$ the whole class star of $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ is refined, and the leaf face that $(i)$ leaves on level $\max(\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone)$ is either carried over or refined further. Those are the two lower bounds of~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar_set}.

    For the upper bound, let $\face{\lone}{\fineIdxone}$ be a leaf face of the output leaf star of $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ on level $\lone$, so $\face{\lone}{\fineIdxone} \in \FVanc{\lone}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})$. If $\face{\lone}{\fineIdxone} \in \M_\lone$ already, then $\M_{\lone+1} \subseteq \M'_{\lone+1}$ makes it a leaf face of the input as well, and hence $\lone \leq \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$. Otherwise the operator adjoined it, so Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} gives a marked class $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo}$ with $\lone \leq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxtwo$ and $\face{\lone}{\fineIdxone} \in \Aop{}{\lone-1}{\lone}(\FVanc{\lone-1}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo}))$. If $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo} = \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$, the first of the two is $\lone \leq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ and there is nothing left to do. Otherwise pass to the parent $\anc{\lone-1}(\face{\lone}{\fineIdxone})$. It lies in $\FVanc{\lone-1}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo})$, because $\anc{\lone-1}$ undoes $\Aop{}{\lone-1}{\lone}$, and in $\FVanc{\lone-1}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})$, because $\anc{}$ composes. The two class stars therefore share a face on level $\lone \! - \! 1$, which gives $\lone \leq \lmeetIdx{\coarseIdxone}{\coarseIdxtwo} \! + \! 1$ by~\eqref{eq:meeting_level} and $\lone \leq \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone} \! + \! 1$ by Lemma~\ref{lemma:meeting_level_leafstar}. Every leaf face of the output leaf star therefore sits on a level at most $\max(\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone} \! + \! 1, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone)$.

    \emph{Clause $(iii)$.} At the target level $\lzero$ the index condition $\l < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxtwo$ of Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} is empty on every level. Adjoining $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo}$ to $\markedVertexClasses$ with that target therefore changes neither the output nor the hypothesis of clause $(ii)$, and both maxima of~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar_set} collapse to their first argument.
\end{proof}

The upper bound of~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar_set} can exceed the lower bound under one condition only. It needs a marked class $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo} \neq \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ with $\lmeetIdx{\coarseIdxone}{\coarseIdxtwo} = \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ and $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxtwo > \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$. The proof delivers the first of the two as $\lmeetIdx{\coarseIdxone}{\coarseIdxtwo} \geq \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$, and Lemma~\ref{lemma:meeting_level_leafstar} closes it to an equality. Read on the mesh, the two classes are still adjacent on level $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$, and $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo}$ is graded past that level. If no marked class meets both requirements, then $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \max(\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone)$, which is the second equality of~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar}.

The condition is attainable. Take $\L = 2$ and a uniformly once refined domain sequence $\domseq[2] = \M_\lzero \sqcup \M_1 \sqcup \varnothing$. Let $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ be a vertex class with a representative $\vertex{\lzero}{\coarseIdxone}$ on level $\lzero$, and let $\vertex{\lzero}{\coarseIdxone}$ be a corner vertex of the face $\face{\lzero}{\fineIdxone}$. Let $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo}$ be a vertex class whose level-$1$ representative $\vertex{1}{\coarseIdxtwo}$ is the midpoint of an edge of $\face{\lzero}{\fineIdxone}$ that touches $\vertex{\lzero}{\coarseIdxone}$. Every leaf face of the input $\domseq[2]$ sits on level $1$, so $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone} = \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxtwo} = 1$. The two classes are adjacent on level $1$, and their birth levels are $\birthLevel(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) = \lzero$ and $\birthLevel(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo}) = 1$, so~\eqref{eq:meeting_level_bound} closes the meeting level to $\lmeetIdx{\coarseIdxone}{\coarseIdxtwo} = 1 = \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$. Set $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone = 1$ and $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxtwo = 2$ and compute $\domseq[2]' = \quadriV(\domseq[2], \{ \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}, \vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo} \}, \targetlevel)$. The lower bound of~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar_set} is $1$. But the child of $\face{\lzero}{\fineIdxone}$ at the corner $\vertex{1}{\coarseIdxone}$ carries $\vertex{1}{\coarseIdxtwo}$ as a vertex, so refining the class star of $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo}$ to $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxtwo = 2$ also puts a level-$2$ leaf face into the leaf star of $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$. Hence $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = 2$ and the upper bound $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone} \! + \! 1 = 2$ is attained. 

Note that the condition asks only that $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxtwo > \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ but not how far past $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ the target sits, so a marked neighbour lifts this leaf star by one level at most, whatever it is graded to. As a result, $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxtwo = 3$ would still leave $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = 2$ in the example.

\smallskip

Corollary~\ref{cor:graded_quadrisection_leafstar} says how the vertex quadrisection operator changes the level spans of the leaf stars of the input domain sequence. The next lemma shows that it achieves it by refining as little as possible, which is the reason for adding the class star and nothing wider.

The next lemma orders domain sequences. For two domain sequences $\domseq[\L], \domseq[\L]' \in \alldomseqs_\L$ of the same $\T_\L$ with regions $\M_\l$ and $\M'_\l$, respectively, we write $\domseq[\L]' \supseteq \domseq[\L]$ when
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:domseq_order}
    \M_\l \; \subseteq \; \M'_\l \qquad \text{for all} \qquad \lzero \leq \l \leq \L .
\end{equation}
Reflexivity, antisymmetry and transitivity are inherited from set inclusion on each level, so this is a partial order. It is not a total one, since two domain sequences that refine different parts of $\T_\lzero$ are incomparable. It says that $\domseq[\L]'$ is at least as refined as $\domseq[\L]$ on every level, and read on the mesh it says that every leaf face of $\domseq[\L]'$ is a descendant of a leaf face of $\domseq[\L]$. A leaf face of $\domseq[\L]'$ on level $\lone$ is not refined beyond level $\lone$ in $\domseq[\L]'$, hence not in $\domseq[\L]$ either, so Corollary~\ref{cor:ancestor_walk} supplies it with a covering face there. It is also the order the adaptive loop of Section~\ref{subsec:adaptive_loop} moves along, since the operators of this section only ever add faces to a region, and the adaptive spaces nest along it by~\eqref{eq:quadrisection_monotone}.

The lemma also needs a name for the kind of refinements it compares. Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$ and let $\markedVertexClasses$ and $\targetlevel$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection}. We call $\domseqAlt[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$ with regions $\MAlt_\l$ a \emph{homogenisation} of $\domseq[\L]$ for $\markedVertexClasses$ and $\targetlevel$ if $\domseqAlt[\L] \supseteq \domseq[\L]$ in the order~\eqref{eq:domseq_order} and
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:homogenisation_constraint}
    \lminAlt \big( \FVleaf{}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \big) \; \geq \; \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \qquad \text{read on } \domseqAlt[\L] \text{ for every } \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \markedVertexClasses.
\end{equation}
Both requirements are lower bounds, so a homogenisation is far from unique. Refining $\domseqAlt[\L]$ any further leaves both of them standing.

\begin{lemma}[The graded vertex quadrisection is the least-refinement homogenisation]
    \label{lemma:graded_quadrisection_minimal}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$ and let $\markedVertexClasses$ and $\targetlevel$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection}. Write $\domseq[\L]' \coloneq \quadriV(\domseq[\L], \markedVertexClasses, \targetlevel)$ with regions $\M'_\l$ for the output of the operator. Then $\domseq[\L]'$ is a homogenisation of $\domseq[\L]$ for $\markedVertexClasses$ and $\targetlevel$, and every homogenisation $\domseqAlt[\L]$ of $\domseq[\L]$ satisfies
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:graded_quadrisection_minimal}
        \domseqAlt[\L] \; \supseteq \; \domseq[\L]' .
    \end{equation}
    The operator is therefore the least element of the homogenisations of $\domseq[\L]$ in the order~\eqref{eq:domseq_order}, and in particular the unique coarsest refinement of $\domseq[\L]$ that homogenises every marked class to its target level.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    The operator is a homogenisation. Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} only adds faces to the regions of $\domseq[\L]$ and never takes any away, so $\domseq[\L]' \supseteq \domseq[\L]$ holds levelwise, which is~\eqref{eq:domseq_order}. For a single marked class, Corollary~\ref{cor:graded_quadrisection_leafstar} shows that $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \max(\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone) \geq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$. Adjoining the remaining marked vertex classes in $\markedVertexClasses$ only grows the regions $\M'_\l$ further, and a leaf face never gets coarser when a region grows, so~\eqref{eq:homogenisation_constraint} holds for the whole marked set.

    The output $\domseq[\L]'$ sits below every homogenisation. Let $\domseqAlt[\L]$ with regions $\MAlt_\l$ be one, let $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \markedVertexClasses$ and let $\l < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$. Every face of the mesh star $\FVl{\L}(\vertex{\L}{\coarseIdxone})$ is covered in $\domseqAlt[\L]$ by a leaf face on a level $\geq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone > \l$ by~\eqref{eq:homogenisation_constraint}, so its level-$\l$ ancestor is refined beyond level $\l$ and hence lies in $\ancestorPatch{\MAlt_{\l+1}}$. Those ancestors are exactly the class star $\FVanc{\l}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})$ by Definition~\ref{def:star_ancestry}, so $\Aop{}{\l}{\l+1}(\FVanc{\l}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})) \subseteq \MAlt_{\l+1}$. Taking the union over the marked classes with $\l < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$, and using $\M_{\l+1} \subseteq \MAlt_{\l+1}$, bounds the region $\M'_{\l+1}$ of~\eqref{eq:graded_vertex_quadrisection} by $\MAlt_{\l+1}$. Level $\lzero$ is $\M_\lzero$ on both sides, so~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_minimal} holds on every level.

    A least element is unique by the antisymmetry of~\eqref{eq:domseq_order}, so no other homogenisation of $\domseq[\L]$ is one.
\end{proof}

Lemma~\ref{lemma:graded_quadrisection_minimal} is a consequence of the refinement operators we constructed. The property would be lost if we chose different refinement operators, for example a non-graded operator, which refines the level-$\lzero$ class star of every marked class uniformly up to its target level and never re-reads the class star in between. It homogenises as well, and it is strictly larger as soon as some target level exceeds $1$ because it keeps all $4$ children of a refined face rather than only the one that touches the vertex class on every level.

\smallskip

The graded vertex quadrisection operator will be used to repair pinches. The following lemma shows that any representative of a vertex class refined to a target level cannot exhibit pinches up to that target level because it has a full refinement fan.

\begin{corollary}[The graded quadrisection spends vertices up to target level]
    \label{cor:pinch_spent}
    Let $\domseqAlt[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$ be any homogenisation of $\domseq[\L]$ for $\markedVertexClasses$ and $\targetlevel$ in the sense of Lemma~\ref{lemma:graded_quadrisection_minimal} and let $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \markedVertexClasses$ be a marked class. Then the refinement fan of $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ in $\domseqAlt[\L]$ is its entire mesh star,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:pinch_spent}
        \refFanAlt{\l} \big( \vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \big) \; = \; \FVl{\l} \big( \vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \big),
    \end{equation}
    on every level $\birthLevel(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \leq \l < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$. In particular $\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone}$ is neither a pinch nor a boundary pinch on such a level. The operator $\quadriV(\domseq[\L], \markedVertexClasses, \targetlevel)$ is such a homogenisation, and so is every domain sequence obtained from it by further quadrisection.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
    The leaf star of $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ read on $\domseqAlt[\L]$ has coarsest level $\lminIdxAlt{\coarseIdxone} \geq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ by~\eqref{eq:homogenisation_constraint}. Every level $\l < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ therefore falls in the first case of~\eqref{eq:class_star_levels}, which refines the whole class star beyond its own level. At the levels at which the vertex exists that class star is the mesh star by~\eqref{eq:star_ancestry_is_star} and the intersection with $\ancestorPatch{\MAlt_{\l+1}}$ is the refinement fan of Definition~\ref{def:refinement_fan}, which is~\eqref{eq:pinch_spent}. The operator is a homogenisation by Lemma~\ref{lemma:graded_quadrisection_minimal}, and a further quadrisection only grows the regions, which leaves both~\eqref{eq:domseq_order} and~\eqref{eq:homogenisation_constraint} standing. A full refinement fan is a single arc and contains both extremes of a linear fan, so neither Definition~\ref{def:pinch_vertex} nor Definition~\ref{def:boundary_pinch} can fire.
\end{proof}

Nothing here forbids $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ from being a pinch on a level $\l \geq \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$, and nothing should. The constraint~\eqref{eq:homogenisation_constraint} is a floor on the leaf star and says nothing about the levels above the target.

\begin{remark}[Why we chose to homogenise the whole class star]
    \label{rem:pinch_repair_choice}
    Repairing a pinch by homogenisation refines more than is technically necessary. To resolve a pinch (cf. Definition~\ref{def:pinch_vertex}), it would be sufficient to close all refinement arcs except for one. Homogenisation closes even that last refinement arc, so it over-refines. Thus, homogenisation is not a minimal repair, only a minimal refinement to raise the leaf stars to their target levels.

    We still choose to homogenise for two reasons, and each of them is a result already proved. A third reason is what it does to the accounting of the pinch iteration, which Section~\ref{subsec:padding} takes up.

    First, homogenisation is canonical. No gap has to be selected, so the outcome depends on the class and the target alone, which is what makes Corollary~\ref{cor:graded_quadrisection_composition} give order-independence and a second application that is the identity. A rule that picks a specific refinement arc might not have either.

    Second, we need no additional operators and machinery. The refinement and the grading steps of the adaptive loop in Section~\ref{subsec:adaptive_loop} ask for homogenisation on their own account, and a homogenised class has the closed-form leaf star~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar} and falls under the three cases of Proposition~\ref{proposition:class_star_levels}. A partially filled fan has no such description, so a partial repair would need machinery that nothing else in this section could reuse.

    The overshoot is bounded from the other side. By Lemma~\ref{lemma:graded_quadrisection_minimal} the operator is the least refinement that homogenises to the target at all, so beyond that one gap per repair nothing is spent. A partial repair would also refine a neighbourhood on one side only, working against the grading step and its purpose of smoothing transitions.
\end{remark}

The partial ordering introduced by~\eqref{eq:domseq_order} coincides with the one used in~\cite{companion_paper} to define the notion of relatively finer domain sequences. Their work proves that
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:quadrisection_monotone}
    \hierSDFspace{k}{\domseq[\L]}{} \subseteq \hierSDFspace{k}{\domseq[\L]'}{} \qquad \text{when} \qquad \domseq[\L]' \supseteq \domseq[\L].
\end{equation}
Since both the face quadrisection operator from Definition~\ref{def:face_quadrisection_operator} and the graded vertex quadrisection operator from Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} respect this ordering, every $\domseq[\L]'$ generated from them gives rise to a superspace $\hierSDFspace{k}{\domseq[\L]'}{}$ of $\hierSDFspace{k}{\domseq[\L]}{}$.

\smallskip

Admissible domain sequences respect the geometric constraints necessary for linear independence and reproducing the correct cohomology, see Definition~\ref{def:subdiv_partition}. The refinement operators $\quadriF(\domseq[\L], \cdot)$ and $\quadriV(\domseq[\L], \cdot, \cdot)$ introduced in this section are maps $\alldomseqs \to \alldomseqs$. However, naive refinement of admissible domain sequences can reintroduce inadmissible geometry, for example a pinch vertex. Thus, admissible domain sequences are not closed under the quadrisection operators, and that their restrictions are maps $\allpartitions \to \alldomseqs$. This is exactly why the following Section~\ref{subsec:adaptive_loop} sets up a padding step that repairs these newly introduced geometric defects to build a map $\allpartitions \to \allpartitions$ that respects the ordering induced by $\supseteq$ from~\eqref{eq:domseq_order}.

\subsection{Locality of the refinement operators}
\label{subsec:refinement_locality}

A quadrisection operator only ever grows the regions of a domain sequence. It therefore cannot destroy a face or a vertex, and every predicate of Section~\ref{subsec:subdiv_partition} can be changed after applying a refinement operator. This section studies how the three predicates of Definitions~\ref{def:confined_face},~\ref{def:pinch_vertex} and~\ref{def:boundary_pinch} and the level span of Definition~\ref{def:level_range} can differ between a domain sequence and its refinement. We consider an arbitrary marked family, nothing below depends on how the marked family was chosen. Section~\ref{subsubsec:locality_candidates} turns the answer into the candidate sets the padding loop passes between its substeps and narrows the results of this section by using additional structure of the specific algorithm.

\smallskip

All three predicates, the pinch vertex, the boundary pinch and the confined face, are properties of the refinement fans of vertices, see Definitions~\ref{def:pinch_vertex},~\ref{def:boundary_pinch} and~\ref{def:confined_face}. Thus, the vertices and faces whose predicates can change are found from the vertices whose refinement fans change after applying a quadrisection operator. Write $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ for the marked family that the quadrisection refines in the sense of~\eqref{eq:marked_face_family} with slices $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$. As an initial coarse split and to state the lemma below, we decompose the whole level-$\l$ mesh into
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:vertex_decomposition}
    \V_\l(\T_\l) \; = \; \big( \V_\l(\T_\l) \setminus \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ) \big) \; \sqcup \; \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ) .
\end{equation}
The set $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$ of~\eqref{eq:newly_refined_slice} contains all faces that the quadrisection actually refines, that is the faces it adds to the ancestor patch on level $\l$ by~\eqref{eq:ancestor_patch_enlargement}. A marked face outside it refines nothing and never changes a refinement fan.

\smallskip

The first part of the decomposition~\eqref{eq:vertex_decomposition} is discharged by the following lemma, which shows that every vertex in it preserves its refinement fan.

\begin{lemma}[Refinement fans are fixed away from refined faces]
    \label{lemma:pad_locality}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$, let $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ with slices $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$ be a marked family for $\domseq[\L]$ in the sense of~\eqref{eq:marked_face_family}, and let $\domseq[\L]' \coloneq \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$ with refinement fans $\refFan{\l}'$. Then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:pad_locality}
        \refFan{\l}' \big( \vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \big) \; = \; \refFan{\l} \big( \vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \big) \qquad \text{for all} \qquad \vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \in \V_\l(\T_\l) \setminus \V_\l \big( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} \big)
    \end{equation}
    holds on every level $\l \in \{ \lzero, \ldots, \L\}$ . Consequently,
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=3pt, itemsep=-2pt]
        \item[$(i)$] a vertex that is neither a pinch nor a boundary pinch of $\domseq[\L]$ on level $\l$ is neither one of $\domseq[\L]'$ on level $\l$, unless it is a vertex of a face of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$, and
        \item[$(ii)$] a leaf face of $\domseq[\L]$ on level $\l$ that is unconfined in $\domseq[\L]$ and is still a leaf face of $\domseq[\L]'$ is unconfined in $\domseq[\L]'$, unless it shares a vertex with a face of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$.
    \end{enumerate}
    Read as sets, clause $(ii)$ says the confinement predicate can change only for the faces of $\FVl{\l} ( \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ) )$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    By Definition~\ref{def:face_quadrisection_operator}, $\quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$ enlarges the region $\M_{\l+1}$ by $\Aop{}{\l}{\l+1}(\simplexsubsetTwo{\l})$ on every level, so it enlarges the ancestor patch $\ancestorPatch{\M_{\l+1}}$ by exactly the slice $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$ and by nothing else, as computed in~\eqref{eq:face_quadrisection_nesting}. The refinement fan $\refFan{\l}(\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone}) = \FVl{\l}(\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone}) \cap \ancestorPatch{\M_{\l+1}}$ of Definition~\ref{def:refinement_fan} can therefore only change at a vertex whose mesh star on level $\l$ meets the enlargement. That enlargement is $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$ and not the whole slice, since a marked face already in $\ancestorPatch{\M_{\l+1}}$ adds nothing to it. So the refinement fan can change only at a vertex of a face of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$, which is~\eqref{eq:pad_locality}. Note that the levels do not interact. The slice $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$ alone decides what changes on level $\l$, whatever the other slices contain. Both consequences follow because the three predicates are read off the refinement fan alone. The pinch of Definition~\ref{def:pinch_vertex} and the boundary pinch of Definition~\ref{def:boundary_pinch} count its arcs, and the confined face of Definition~\ref{def:confined_face} asks whether one of its vertices has an empty fan.
\end{proof}

The inclusion is sharp. If a face of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$ is incident to a vertex, it enters the refinement fan of that vertex read on the output domain sequence. Thus, the refinement fan does move there.

Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality} is quantified over the whole mesh $\V_\l(\T_\l)$, so it reaches every vertex outside $\V_\l(\simplexsubsetNew{\l})$ and fixes the refinement fan there. What it does not deliver is a reading of a predicate on $\domseq[\L]'$ for an object that only exists in $\domseq[\L]'$ but not in $\domseq[\L]$. The face quadrisection operator only grows regions, so $\domseq[\L]'$ carries vertices on level $\l$ that $\domseq[\L]$ did not carry, and it adds the children of the refined faces as new leaf faces. Every object of $\domseq[\L]'$ therefore falls into one of three cases. It is \emph{untouched} when it is present in both and keeps its refinement fan, which Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality} gives to every vertex outside $\V_\l(\simplexsubsetNew{\l})$. It has a \emph{changed state} when it is present in both and its refinement fan has moved. It is a \emph{new object} when $\domseq[\L]'$ carries it and $\domseq[\L]$ does not and thus has no reading on $\domseq[\L]$ to be compared with. Only the last two cases can change their predicates on $\domseq[\L]'$ compared to the input $\domseq[\L]$. We use the three names throughout this section.

Three splits follow, one for the vertices, one for the leaf faces and one for the vertex classes. Each of them cuts its objects into the three cases above and tags them $(\mathrm{U})$ untouched, $(\mathrm{C})$ changed and $(\mathrm{N})$ new. A tag names the same case in all three splits. Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality} above fixes the untouched objects $(\mathrm{U})$ for the two refinement fan predicates and Lemma~\ref{lemma:leafstar_locality} does it for the level span. Lemma~\ref{lemma:new_objects} settles the new objects $(\mathrm{N})$, emptying them at the vertices and bounding them at the faces. Lemma~\ref{lemma:span_opens} handles the changed objects $(\mathrm{C})$ for the level span, which is the one predicate that a locality statement alone does not decide.

For vertices the two cases split the level-$\l$ vertices of the output. We do not aim for a finer split of~\eqref{eq:vertex_decomposition} but consider ambient set $\V_\l(\M'_\l)$ instead of $\V_\l(\T_\l)$, since a predicate is read on the region and not on the whole mesh. This gives
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:vertex_decomposition_output}
    \V_\l(\M'_\l) \; = \; \underbrace{\big( \V_\l(\M_\l) \setminus \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ) \big)}_{(\mathrm{U}) \text{ untouched}} \; \sqcup \; \underbrace{\V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} )}_{(\mathrm{C}) \text{ changed}} \; \sqcup \; \underbrace{\big( \V_\l(\M'_\l) \setminus \V_\l(\M_\l) \big) \setminus \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} )}_{(\mathrm{N})\text{ new}} ,
\end{equation}
where the third part is carried by the children of the faces refined one level coarser,
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:new_vertices_are_children}
    \big( \V_\l(\M'_\l) \setminus \V_\l(\M_\l) \big) \; \subseteq \; \V_\l \big( \Aop{}{\l-1}{\l} ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l-1} ) \big) .
\end{equation}
Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality} fixes the predicates on the untouched vertices $(\mathrm{U})$. The changed vertices $(\mathrm{C})$ are the ones at which a predicate can have changed, and Section~\ref{subsubsec:locality_candidates} collects them. Lemma~\ref{lemma:new_objects} discharges the new vertices $(\mathrm{N})$, the ones the refinement adds. Figure~\ref{fig:vertex_split} shows the three parts on one marked family, level by level.

% ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

For faces the same two cases split the leaf faces of the output domain sequence since Definition~\ref{def:confined_face} reads the confinement predicate on their refinement fans. The three parts arise differently than they do for the vertices, but they are the same three roles. A face of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$ is refined and stops being a leaf face, so it leaves the ambient set instead of forming the changed states. What changes state are the leaf faces around it, which clause $(ii)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality} places in the one-ring $\FVl{\l} ( \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ) )$. What is new are the children that the level below leaves behind. Written out this becomes
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:leaf_face_decomposition_output}
    \begin{split}
        \leafF{\l} \big( \domseq[\L]' \big) \; = \;\;\;\;\;\; & \underbrace{\Big( \leafF{\l}(\domseq[\L]) \setminus \FVl{\l} \big( \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ) \big) \Big)}_{(\mathrm{U}) \text{ untouched}}  \\[4pt]
        \sqcup \; & \underbrace{\Big( \leafF{\l}(\domseq[\L]) \cap \FVl{\l} \big( \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ) \big) \Big) \setminus \simplexsubsetNew{\l}}_{(\mathrm{C}) \text{ changed}} \sqcup \;\; \underbrace{\Aop{}{\l-1}{\l} \big( \simplexsubsetNew{\l-1} \big) \setminus \simplexsubsetNew{\l}}_{(\mathrm{N}) \text{ new}} .
    \end{split}
\end{equation}
The quadrisection operator adds exactly $\Aop{}{\l-1}{\l}(\simplexsubsetNew{\l-1})$ to $\M_\l$ by~\eqref{eq:face_quadrisection_nesting} and exactly $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$ to $\ancestorPatch{\M_{\l+1}}$ by~\eqref{eq:ancestor_patch_enlargement}. The untouched and the changed faces together are the leaf faces of $\domseq[\L]$ that survive. A face of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$ lies in the one-ring of its own vertices, so $(\mathrm{C})$ removes it and $(\mathrm{U})$ never carries it.

The untouched faces $(\mathrm{U})$ do not meet any refinement and clause $(ii)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality} fixes their predicate. The new faces cannot be discharged the way the new vertices are. Clause $(ii)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:new_objects} below only narrows them. Figure~\ref{fig:face_split} shows the three parts on one marked family and draws the faces of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$ that leave the ambient set level by level.

Removing $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$ from the new faces $(\mathrm{N})$ is not vacuous. A marked family may carry a face on level $\l\!-\!1$ and one of its children on level $\l$, which~\eqref{eq:marked_face_family} allows on purpose because the graded vertex quadrisection needs it. A child born on level $\l$ can then be refined again by the same application and never appears as a leaf face of $\domseq[\L]'$. For a marking by leaf faces of $\domseq[\L]$ the removal is empty and~\eqref{eq:leaf_face_decomposition_output} is the split into surviving leaf faces and children alone. The two outlined faces inside the new-face block $(\mathrm{N})$ on level $1$ of Figure~\ref{fig:face_split} mark a removal of this kind.

%

% mesh's own edge instead, since a filled face is big enough to need no rim.
%
% Fills, rim and all, are those of Figure~\ref{fig:vertex_split} exactly.

% ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

\smallskip

The next lemma discards new vertices as sites at which a predicate can change after the refinement, and thus narrows where the padding of Section~\ref{subsec:padding} has to look. Its second clause treats the faces. There the new objects cannot be discarded, since a new face can be confined.

\begin{lemma}[Predicates at new objects]
    \label{lemma:new_objects}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$ with regions $\M_\l$, let $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ be a marked family for $\domseq[\L]$, and let $\domseq[\L]' \coloneq \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$ with regions $\M'_\l$ and refinement fans $\refFan{\l}'$.
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=3pt, itemsep=-2pt]
        \item[$(i)$] A vertex the refinement adds on a level carries no refinement fan there, unless the slice of that level refines at it. That is,
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:new_vertex_empty_fan}
            \refFan{\l}' \big( \vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \big) \; = \; \varnothing \qquad \text{for all} \qquad \vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \in \big( \V_\l(\M'_\l) \setminus \V_\l(\M_\l) \big) \setminus \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ) .
        \end{equation}
        In particular no such vertex is a pinch vertex or a boundary pinch of $\domseq[\L]'$ on level $\l$, so neither predicate holds at the new vertices $(\mathrm{N})$ of~\eqref{eq:vertex_decomposition_output}.
        \item[$(ii)$] A face $\face{\l+1}{\fineIdxone} \in \Aop{}{\l}{\l+1}(\simplexsubsetNew{\l})$ the refinement adds and that is confined in $\domseq[\L]'$ satisfies 
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:confined_new_face_no_part_C}
            \VFl{\l+1}(\face{\l+1}{\fineIdxone}) \subseteq \V_{\l+1}(\M_{\l+1}) \cup \V_{\l+1} ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l+1} )
        \end{equation}
        and thus none of its vertices lie in the new vertices $(\mathrm{N})$ of~\eqref{eq:vertex_decomposition_output}. That is, every vertex of $\face{\l+1}{\fineIdxone}$ either was already carried by $\domseq[\L]$ on level $\l\!+\!1$ or is one at which the slice $\simplexsubsetNew{\l+1}$ refines.
    \end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    For clause $(i)$ note first that the refinement fan of such a vertex is empty on the input domain sequence. A face of $\refFan{\l}(\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone})$ lies in $\ancestorPatch{\M_{\l+1}} \subseteq \M_\l$ by Definition~\ref{def:refinement_fan} and would put the vertex into $\V_\l(\M_\l)$. Now~\eqref{eq:pad_locality} is quantified over the whole level-$\l$ mesh $\V_\l(\T_\l)$ and not only over the vertices of the regions, so it applies at $\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone}$ and carries the empty fan over to the output, which is~\eqref{eq:new_vertex_empty_fan}. An empty refinement fan carries no refinement arc at all, while Definition~\ref{def:pinch_vertex} asks for two arcs and Definition~\ref{def:boundary_pinch} for one, so neither predicate fires.

    For clause $(ii)$ note first that the face lies in $\M'_{\l+1}$, so its vertices are split by~\eqref{eq:vertex_decomposition_output} on level $\l\!+\!1$. The union on the right of~\eqref{eq:confined_new_face_no_part_C} is exactly the untouched and the changed vertices of that split. The inclusion and the statement about the new vertices are therefore the same claim. The mechanism is that the three parts of~\eqref{eq:vertex_decomposition_output} answer Definition~\ref{def:refinement_exterior_vertex} of refinement exteriority on level $\l\!+\!1$ differently. A new vertex $(\mathrm{N})$ carries an empty fan by clause $(i)$ read on that level, so it is refinement-exterior. A changed vertex $(\mathrm{C})$ lies on a face of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l+1}$, and the refinement adjoins that face to $\ancestorPatch{\M'_{\l+2}}$ by~\eqref{eq:ancestor_patch_enlargement}. Thus, its fan on level $\l\!+\!1$ contains that face and it is not refinement-exterior. An untouched vertex $(\mathrm{U})$ meets no face of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l+1}$, so~\eqref{eq:pad_locality} hands it the fan it carried on the input, which may be empty or not. The new vertices are therefore the only part of the three that is refinement-exterior throughout. A leaf face with a refinement-exterior vertex on its own level is unconfined by Definition~\ref{def:confined_face}, so a confined $\face{\l+1}{\fineIdxone}$ has no new vertex, which is~\eqref{eq:confined_new_face_no_part_C}.
\end{proof}

The asymmetry between the two clauses of Lemma~\ref{lemma:new_objects} is worth addressing, since it decides which of the new objects $(\mathrm{N})$ can still carry a predicate. Refining on level $\l$ leaves every refinement fan on level $\l\!+\!1$ untouched, since those fans read $\ancestorPatch{\M_{\l+2}}$ and~\eqref{eq:ancestor_patch_enlargement} does not reach it. Both kinds of new object therefore sit on fan data that did not move, and the only question is whether their predicate can be true on it. A new vertex has an empty fan of its own, and both pinch predicates are false on an empty fan. In contrast, a new face reads the fans of three vertices instead of one, and it can consist entirely of vertices the input already carried, in which case nothing forces any of them to be refinement-exterior and the face can be confined. So the new objects are vacuous for vertices but not for faces. Looking at the two splits, clause $(i)$ discharges the new vertices while clause $(ii)$ only bounds the vertices of the new faces but leaves them standing. Lemma~\ref{lemma:confined_children} of Section~\ref{subsubsec:confined_faces} shows a configuration that realises it, drawn in the confinement column of Figure~\ref{fig:candidate_sets}.

\smallskip

The grading step of Section~\ref{subsubsec:grading} uses the level span of Definition~\ref{def:level_range} as its predicate. Since that is not a refinement fan predicate, the lemma above does not reach it. It is read off the leaf star, and the leaf star has a locality statement of its own. We give it here, and name the faces a quadrisection actually refines on the way.

Vertex classes admit a similar split to~\eqref{eq:vertex_decomposition_output} for vertices and~\eqref{eq:leaf_face_decomposition_output} for leaf faces. The ambient set is $\vertexClasses(\domseq[\L]')$ and not a set of one level, since a class carries a representative on every level from its birth level to $\L$. Its predicate is read off the leaf star rather than a refinement fan, so the criterion cannot name a single slice. We collect the faces the quadrisection refines for the first time over all levels,
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:newly_refined_family}
    \simplexsubsetNewAll{} \; \coloneq \; \bigsqcup\limits_{\l = \lzero}^{\L-1} \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ,
\end{equation}
and split the classes into the same three roles. Written out this becomes
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:vertex_class_decomposition_output}
    \begin{split}
        \vertexClasses \big( \domseq[\L]' \big) \; = \;\;\;\;\;\; & \underbrace{\big\{ \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L]) \; : \; \FVleaf{} \big( \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \big) \cap \simplexsubsetNewAll{} \, = \, \varnothing \big\}}_{(\mathrm{U}) \text{ untouched}} \\[4pt]
        \sqcup \; & \underbrace{\big\{ \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L]) \; : \; \FVleaf{} \big( \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \big) \cap \simplexsubsetNewAll{} \, \neq \, \varnothing \big\}}_{(\mathrm{C}) \text{ changed}} \; \sqcup \;\; \underbrace{\vertexClasses(\domseq[\L]') \setminus \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])}_{(\mathrm{N}) \text{ new}} .
    \end{split}
\end{equation}
The face quadrisection operator only grows regions, so $\vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])$ is contained in $\vertexClasses(\domseq[\L]')$ and the three parts do partition it. The untouched classes $(\mathrm{U})$ experience no refinement and Lemma~\ref{lemma:leafstar_locality} below fixes their leaf star, and with it the three quantities of Definition~\ref{def:level_range}. Its criterion is the right side of~\eqref{eq:leafstar_locality}. The changed classes $(\mathrm{C})$ need a step beyond that locality, because their predicate is not read off a refinement fan but off the level span. Lemma~\ref{lemma:span_opens} below provides it. The new classes $(\mathrm{N})$ are the ones the refinement creates. Figure~\ref{fig:class_split} shows the three parts on the marked family of Figure~\ref{fig:vertex_split}.
