\begin{lemma}[A quadrisection moves a leaf star only where it refines]
    \label{lemma:leafstar_locality}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$ with regions $\M_\l$, let $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ be a marked family for $\domseq[\L]$, and let $\domseq[\L]' \coloneq \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$. Write ${\FVleaf{}}'$ for the leaf star read on $\domseq[\L]'$. Then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:leafstar_locality}
        {\FVleaf{}}' \big( \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \big) \; = \; \FVleaf{} \big( \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \big)
        \quad \Longleftrightarrow \quad
        \FVleaf{} \big( \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \big) \cap \simplexsubsetNewAll{} \; = \; \varnothing
    \end{equation}
    for every $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])$. In that case $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}$, $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ and $\levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}' = \levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    The mesh star $\FVl{\L}(\vertex{\L}{\coarseIdxone})$ does not depend on the regions, so by Definition~\ref{def:leaf_star} both leaf stars collect the covering faces of the same fine faces on level $\L$. A leaf face of $\domseq[\L]$ stops being one on $\domseq[\L]'$ exactly when it lies in $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$, which is what the untouched and the changed faces of~\eqref{eq:leaf_face_decomposition_output} remove, and every leaf face that survives still covers what it covered before. The leaf star thus changes precisely when it contains one of those faces, which is~\eqref{eq:leafstar_locality}. The three quantities of Definition~\ref{def:level_range} are read off the leaf star alone, so they agree whenever it does.
\end{proof}

Both lemmas above concern the face quadrisection operator. However, by Corollary~\ref{cor:graded_is_face_quadrisection}, the vertex quadrisection operator $\quadriV(\cdot, \markedVertexClasses, \targetlevel)$ is a face quadrisection on the family~\eqref{eq:graded_marked_family} of class stars. Thus, the lemmas are also applicable to the vertex quadrisection operators. On level $\l$ the slice $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$ is then the union of the class stars $\FVanc{\l}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})$ over the marked classes with $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone > \l$, and the predicates that can have changed on level $\l$ are those of its vertices.

\smallskip

The level span needs one statement beyond the two lemmas above. Locality says where a leaf star can change at all. A leaf star can change without its span growing, and what the grading of Section~\ref{subsubsec:grading} tests is the span. The lemma below has three clauses, one for each way a refinement arrives. Clause $(i)$ is unconditional and asks only that the refinement refine no face the class star carries at the fine end of the leaf star. Clause $(ii)$ is the marking by confined faces, where the predicate supplies that condition on its own. A confined face has no refinement-exterior vertex on its own level by Definition~\ref{def:confined_face}, so it never sits at the finest level of a class star. Clause $(iii)$ is the graded vertex quadrisection, which can open a span in a single configuration. That is Corollary~\ref{cor:graded_quadrisection_leafstar} under the restriction that no class is graded past the finest level of its own leaf star.

\begin{lemma}[How refinement moves a level span]
    \label{lemma:span_opens}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$, let $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])$ and write $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}'$, $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}'$ and $\levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}'$ for its leaf star quantities read on $\domseq[\L]'$.
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=3pt, itemsep=-2pt]
        \item[$(i)$] If $\domseq[\L]' = \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$ for an arbitrary marked family $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ with slices $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$ and the class star of $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ carries no newly refined face on the finest level of its leaf star, i.e.
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:span_stays}
            \FVanc{\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}} ( \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} ) \; \cap \; \big( \simplexsubsetNew{\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}} \big) \; = \; \varnothing ,
        \end{equation}
        then
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:face_span_bounds}
            \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \; \geq \; \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \qquad \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \; = \; \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \qquad  \levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}' \; \leq \; \levelSpan{\coarseIdxone} .
        \end{equation}
        \item[$(ii)$] If $\domseq[\L]' = \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$ for a set $\simplexsubsetTwo{} \subseteq \leafF{}(\domseq[\L])$ such that every $\face{\l}{\fineIdxone} \in \simplexsubsetTwo{}$ is confined in $\domseq[\L]$, then the hypothesis~\eqref{eq:span_stays} holds at every $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])$, and clause $(i)$ provides the bounds of~\eqref{eq:face_span_bounds} at each of them.
        \item[$(iii)$] If $\domseq[\L]' = \quadriV(\domseq[\L], \markedVertexClasses, \targetlevel)$ as in Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection}, and $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \leq \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ in case $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \markedVertexClasses$, then
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:span_bounds}
            \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \; \geq \; \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \qquad \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \; \leq \; \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone} \! + \! 1, \qquad \levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}' \; \leq \; \levelSpan{\coarseIdxone} \! + \! 1 ,
        \end{equation}
        and realising the upper bound $\levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}' = \levelSpan{\coarseIdxone} + 1$ forces a marked class $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxtwo} \in \markedVertexClasses \setminus \{ \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \}$ with
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:span_opens}
            \lmeetIdx{\coarseIdxone}{\coarseIdxtwo} \; = \; \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}
            \qquad \text{and} \qquad
            \targetlevel_\coarseIdxtwo \; > \; \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}.
        \end{equation}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    For clause $(i)$ note that Lemma~\ref{lemma:leafstar_locality} is stated for an arbitrary marked family. The leaf star read on $\domseq[\L]'$ therefore replaces every face of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$ it carries by children on level $\l \! + \! 1$ and carries every other face over. That is~\eqref{eq:leaf_face_decomposition_output} read inside the leaf star. The untouched and the changed faces are carried over and the new faces hold the children. Both leaf stars collect the covering faces of the same mesh star $\FVl{\L}(\vertex{\L}{\coarseIdxone})$ by Definition~\ref{def:leaf_star}, and a quadrisection only refines, so no covering face moves to a coarser level. Hence $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \geq \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ and $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' \geq \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ hold with no hypothesis at all, and only the upper bound on the fine end needs~\eqref{eq:span_stays}.

    Suppose the covering face of some $\face{\L}{\fineIdxtwo} \in \FVl{\L}(\vertex{\L}{\coarseIdxone})$ sat above level $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ on $\domseq[\L]'$. Its ancestor $\anc{\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}}(\face{\L}{\fineIdxtwo})$ would then be refined in $\domseq[\L]'$ and lie in $\ancestorPatch{\M'_{\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}+1}}$, which~\eqref{eq:ancestor_patch_enlargement} splits into $\ancestorPatch{\M_{\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}+1}}$ and $\simplexsubsetNew{\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}}$. The first refines that ancestor in $\domseq[\L]$ already and pushes the covering face of $\face{\L}{\fineIdxtwo}$ past $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ there, which Definition~\ref{def:level_range} forbids. The second is excluded by~\eqref{eq:span_stays}, since the ancestor lies in the class star $\FVanc{\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})$ by Definition~\ref{def:star_ancestry}. So no covering face exceeds $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$, which gives $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}' = \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$. Subtracting the two ends gives the level span and with it~\eqref{eq:face_span_bounds}.

    For clause $(ii)$ let $\face{\l}{\fineIdxone} \in \simplexsubsetTwo{}$ be a marked face lying in the class star $\FVanc{\l}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})$. It is enough to show $\l < \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ for every such face, since that is what~\eqref{eq:span_stays} asks.

    If $\birthLevel(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \leq \l$, then $\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone}$ exists and the class star is the mesh star $\FVl{\l}(\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone})$ by~\eqref{eq:star_ancestry_is_star}, so $\face{\l}{\fineIdxone}$ carries $\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone}$. A confined face has no refinement-exterior vertex on its own level by Definition~\ref{def:confined_face}, so Proposition~\ref{proposition:class_star_levels} gives $\l < \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$. If instead $\birthLevel(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) > \l$, then $\birthLevel(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \geq \l \! + \! 1$, and the same proposition bounds $\birthLevel(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \leq \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ because $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ has a representative in $\V(\domseq[\L])$. In both cases, $\l \! + \! 1 \leq \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ follows.

    For clause $(iii)$ the hypothesis $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone \leq \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ removes the target level from both maxima of~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar_set}, and~\eqref{eq:graded_quadrisection_leafstar_unmarked} reads that way at an unmarked class already. Subtracting the two turns them into~\eqref{eq:span_bounds}. The coarsest level never falls, so a level span that grows has to raise the finest level past $\max(\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}, \, \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone)$ and the paragraph after Corollary~\ref{cor:graded_quadrisection_leafstar} shows that this needs exactly the configuration of~\eqref{eq:span_opens}. Both ends are levels, so the level span is an integer, and $\levelSpan{\coarseIdxone} \! + \! 1$ is the only value above $\levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}$ that does not violate~\eqref{eq:span_bounds}.
\end{proof}

The hypothesis~\eqref{eq:span_stays} is read on the class star and not on the leaf star. A marked family may refine a face and one of its own children in a single application, which~\eqref{eq:marked_face_family} allows and the graded vertex quadrisection uses. A face born on level $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ and refined again by the same application was never a leaf face of $\domseq[\L]$, so a leaf star reading would not see it, while it still drives $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ up by one. The class star sees it, because it carries every level-$\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ ancestor of the mesh star whether or not the region reaches that level. This is the configuration~\eqref{eq:span_opens} of clause $(iii)$, so the two clauses agree where they overlap.

The three splits and the four results above fill nine cells. Each row is one split and each column is one of the three cases.
\begin{center}
    \small
    \begin{tabular}{@{}l@{\hspace{1.6em}}l@{\hspace{1.6em}}l@{\hspace{1.6em}}l@{}}
        \toprule
        & untouched $(\mathrm{U})$ & changed $(\mathrm{C})$ & new $(\mathrm{N})$ \\
        \midrule
        vertices     & Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality}      & must be tested & Lemma~\ref{lemma:new_objects}~$(i)$, empty \\
        leaf faces   & Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality}~$(ii)$ & must be tested & must be tested, bounded by~$(ii)$ \\
        vertex classes & Lemma~\ref{lemma:leafstar_locality} & Lemma~\ref{lemma:span_opens} & must be tested \\
        \bottomrule
    \end{tabular}
\end{center}
The untouched objects $(\mathrm{U})$ are fixed by a locality statement and need no test. The changed objects $(\mathrm{C})$ have no such statement and have to be tested everywhere. So do the new objects $(\mathrm{N})$, except at the vertices, where they are empty.

The three splits are what a refinement hands on. Each one names, for a single kind of object, the places at which a predicate can change after refinement. The untouched part never needs a test again. What remains is the changed part, together with the new part at the leaf faces and at the vertex classes. Section~\ref{subsubsec:locality_candidates} collects those into three maps and shows that the maps catch every predicate a refinement can change.

\FloatBarrier

\section{Implementation and adaptive loop}
\label{subsec:adaptive_loop}

This section turns the constructions of Sections~\ref{sec:construction_of_hierarchical_subdiv_spaces} and~\ref{sec:refinement_operators} into a working adaptive algorithm. Given an admissible domain sequence $\domseq[\L]$, the active DoFs $\activeDoFSet{k}{}(\domseq[\L])$ follow from Definition~\ref{def:active_k_form_dofs} level by level. The adaptive subdivision matrix $\Amat{k}{\activeDoFs}{\L}$ collects the corresponding columns of $\Amat{k}{\l}{\L}$ block by block as in Equation~\eqref{eq:hierarchical_matrix_blocks}. What remains is to embed this machinery in a standard \textsc{Solve}--\textsc{Estimate}--\textsc{Mark}--\textsc{Refine}--\textsc{Pad} adaptive loop. The additional \textsc{Pad} step restores the domain sequence output by \textsc{Refine} to an admissible one after each refinement, see Definition~\ref{def:subdiv_partition}. It does so by iterating pinch resolution, confinement padding, and grading.

The loop used in the numerical experiments consists of the steps
\begin{equation}
\begin{tikzcd}
    \textsc{Solve} \arrow[r] & \textsc{Estimate} \arrow[r] & \textsc{Mark} \arrow[r] & \textsc{Refine} \arrow[r] & \textsc{Pad} \arrow[llll, rounded corners=0pt, to path={(\tikztostart.south) -- ([yshift=-1.em]\tikztostart.south) -- ([yshift=-1.em]\tikztotarget.south) -- (\tikztotarget.south)}]
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}

and is initialized with the admissible domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^0 = \F_\lzero(\T_\lzero) \sqcup \bigsqcup_{\l = 1}^{\L} \varnothing$ on the initial mesh $\T_\lzero$. Throughout the adaptive loop, the general picture of the computational objects in Section~\ref{subsec:computational_representations} stays the same. The loop only refines $\domseq[\L]$ iteratively, which in turn refines the active DoFs $\activeDoFSet{k}{}(\domseq[\L])$ and the adaptive subdivision $k$-form spaces $\hierSDFspace{k}{\domseq}{\L}$. The \textsc{Pad} step is necessary because the \textsc{Refine} step only guarantees $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}} \in \guidance{\alldomseqs}$, not $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}} \in \allpartitions$, see Definition~\ref{def:subdiv_partition}. This means that the \textsc{Pad} step effectively restores linear independence and exactness of the complex by preserving admissibility throughout the adaptive loop, see Corollary~\ref{cor:adaptive_loop_admissible}.

\subsection{Solve}
\label{subsec:solve}

The details of the \textsc{Solve} step are typically problem-specific. The common thread for methods using subdivision differential forms is a two stage assembly. The bilinear forms of the weak formulation are first assembled using the spaces $\FEECspace{k}{\L}$ on $\T_\L$. They are then unrefined to the spaces $\hierSDFspace{k}{\domseq}{\L}$ with the adaptive subdivision matrices $\Amat{k}{\activeDoFs}{\L}$ as in Equation~\eqref{eq:unrefinement} in Section~\ref{subsec:unrefinement_and_solve}. The matrices of $\FEECspace{k}{\L}$ do not change during the adaptive loop because $\T_\L$ is fixed. A step changes only the selection of active DoFs, so its cost is the recomputation of the active DoFs $\activeDoFSet{k}{}$ followed by one sparse triple product per bilinear form. The unrefined matrices of the adaptive space inherit the density and the conditioning from the subdivision basis, which is where the cost of the method reappears. Section~\ref{subsubsec:cl_performance} measures both for an adaptive algorithm solving the mixed vector Laplace problem from Section~\ref{subsec:vector_laplace} and compares them to the reference run using $\FEECspace{k}{\L}$.

\subsection{Estimation}
\label{subsec:estimate}

We compute error indicators $\eta_{\l,\coarseIdxone}$ per leaf face $\face{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \in  \leafF{}(\domseq[\L])$ of the domain sequence $\domseq[\L]$. The residual is evaluated on the finest level $\L$ and then aggregated to the coarse parent faces.
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:project_estimator_on_L_to_partition}
    \eta^2_{\l,\coarseIdxone} = \sum\limits_{\face{\L}{\fineIdxone} \; \in \; \Aop{}{\l}{\L}(\face{\l}{\coarseIdxone})} \eta^2_{\L,\fineIdxone},
\end{equation}
where $\eta^2_{\L,\fineIdxone}$ is evaluated in $\FEECspace{k}{\L}$. Any indicator that can be evaluated per face of $\T_\L$ drives the loop through Equation~\eqref{eq:project_estimator_on_L_to_partition}. The experiments of Section~\ref{subsec:vector_laplace} use the residual indicator of~\cite{Demlow.2014} for the mixed vector Laplace problem, stated for that test case in Equation~\eqref{eq:cl_estimator}.

Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline_marking_a} shows an example domain sequence with residual $\eta^2_{\l,\coarseIdxone}$. In the following, we will refer to $\eta^2$ and $\rho^2$ as the \emph{total} and \emph{refinable} residuals, respectively, defined as
\begin{equation}
    \label{def:total_and_refinable_residuals}
    \eta^2 = \sum\limits_{\l=\lzero}^{\L} \; \eta^2_\l \qquad \text{and} \qquad \rho^2 = \sum\limits_{\l=\lzero}^{\L-1} \; \eta^2_\l = \eta^2 - \eta^2_{\L} \qquad \text{with} \qquad \eta^2_{\l} = \sum\limits_{\face{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \; \in \; \F(\domseq_\l)} \eta^2_{\l,\coarseIdxone}.
\end{equation}
The refinable residual discards the contributions of faces $\face{\L}{\coarseIdxone}$ on level $\L$ of the admissible domain sequence $\domseq$. We measure this refinable residual because faces cannot be refined beyond level $\L$ without global mesh refinement to extend the mesh hierarchy. For this reason, the residual accumulated on level $\L$ can no longer be decreased. In fact, we find that
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:saturation_rate}
    \rho^2 / \eta^2 = 1 - \eta^2_\L / \eta^2 \eqqcolon \xi
\end{equation}
This \emph{saturation rate} $\xi \in [0,1]$ is the fraction of the overall estimator $\eta$ that further refinement can still decrease. It measures the domain sequence against the hierarchy, not against the exact solution. A small $\xi$ says that the accuracy is now limited by $\L$ rather than by the domain sequence, and that extending the hierarchy is the only way to reduce the errors meaningfully. It is therefore a diagnostic and not a stopping criterion. Driving $\xi$ down demands that almost every face reach level $\L$, which is uniform refinement, so a run stopped on $\xi$ spends its last steps adding degrees of freedom that the indicator never asked for.

\subsection{Marking and refinement}
\label{subsec:mark_refine}

\label{subsec:mark}
\label{subsec:refine}

The estimator of Section~\ref{subsec:estimate} is independent of how the domain sequence is refined. What the adaptive loop still has to choose is what kinds of neighbourhoods it refines. Given the quadrisection operators constructed in Section~\ref{subsec:refinement_operators}, there are two natural options. The first is a single leaf face, refined by the face quadrisection $\quadriF$. The second is a vertex star, refined by the graded vertex quadrisection $\quadriV$. In the following, we will refer to these qualitative neighbourhoods as the \emph{atoms} of the refinement step. The padding step of Section~\ref{subsec:padding} needs both operators regardless, because the two repair different defects. Neither choice of atom therefore costs a new construction. The choice is therefore free, and Section~\ref{subsubsec:cl_performance} compares the two in terms of their DoF efficiency and their padding overhead.

\newcommand{\refinableFaces}{\leafF{<\L}}
The following two subsections introduce both variants. What is shared between them is how they select atoms by aggregating residuals of refinable faces. The \emph{refinable faces} are all leaf faces below level $\L$, which we will denote by $\leafF{<\L} \coloneq \leafMesh(\domseq[\L]) \setminus \F_\L(\T_\L)$. Consider a set $\B \subseteq \refinableFaces$ of refinable leaf faces and the refinable residual 
\begin{equation}
    \rho^2_{\B} \coloneq \sum\limits_{\face{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \in \B} \eta^2_{\l, \coarseIdxone}.
\end{equation}
We call $\B_\theta$ a \emph{bulk set} for the D\"orfler parameter $\theta \in (0,1]$ when
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:doerfler_selection}
    \rho_{\B_\theta} \; \geq \; \theta \, \rho ,
\end{equation}
with $\rho$ the refinable residual of Equation~\eqref{def:total_and_refinable_residuals}. The two refinement variants presented in the following differ only in how they assemble $\B_\theta$ and in which operator refines the marked atoms.

\subsubsection{Face refinement}
\label{subsubsec:refine_face}

The first variant is the usual D\"orfler marking of individual faces. The markable faces are the refinable leaf faces $\refinableFaces$ enumerated in decreasing order by their indicators $\eta^2_{\l,\coarseIdxone}$. Dörfler marking takes the first $m$ of them,
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:marked_faces}
    \B_\theta \; \coloneq \; \big\{ \face{\l_1}{\coarseIdxone_1}, \, \dots, \, \face{\l_m}{\coarseIdxone_m} \big\} \; \subseteq \; \refinableFaces ,
\end{equation}
for the smallest $m$ making $\B_\theta$ a bulk set in the sense of~\eqref{eq:doerfler_selection}.

The \textsc{Refine} step is then one application of the face quadrisection operator of Definition~\ref{def:face_quadrisection_operator} to the marked family $\B_\theta$ of leaf faces,
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:refine_faces}
    \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}} \; \coloneq \; \quadriF \big( \domseq[\L], \, \B_\theta \big) ,
\end{equation}
which refines every marked face by exactly one level. The refined domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}$ is the input for the padding step presented in Section~\ref{subsec:padding}.

\FloatBarrier

\subsubsection{Vertex-star refinement}
\label{subsubsec:refine_vertex}

The second variant of the \textsc{Refine} step takes the vertex star as its atom. It ranks vertex classes rather than leaf faces and then refines vertex class stars like in Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline_marking}. The per-face indicators of Equation~\eqref{eq:project_estimator_on_L_to_partition} of Panel~\ref{fig:pipeline_marking_a} are aggregated onto vertex classes first, as shown in Panel~\ref{fig:pipeline_marking_b}. This design choice has one particular advantage. A homogenised star comes out with a full refinement fan, so the marked vertex class cannot itself be a pinch. The padding step of Section~\ref{subsec:padding} then has that much less to repair. Further, it provides important combinatorial constaints to bound the number of passes of the padding loop in Section~\ref{subsec:termination}. A detailed comparison with the face refinement in Section~\ref{subsubsec:refine_face} is presented in Section~\ref{subsubsec:cl_performance}, which measures how much less.

\newcommand{\vertClassResidual}[1]{\nu_{#1}}
The residual $\vertClassResidual{\coarseIdxone}$ of the vertex class $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])$ defined as the area-weighted average
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:vertex_residual}
    \nu^2_\coarseIdxone = \tfrac{1}{a_\coarseIdxone} \sum\limits_{\face{\l}{\fineIdxone} \; \in \; \FVleaf{<\L}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})} \eta^2_{\l,\fineIdxone},
    \qquad \mathrm{where} \qquad
    a_\coarseIdxone = \sum\limits_{\face{\l}{\fineIdxone} \; \in \; \FVleaf{<\L}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone})} \mathrm{area}(\face{\l}{\fineIdxone})
\end{equation}
and $\FVleaf{<\L}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \coloneq \FVleaf{}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}) \setminus \F_\L(\T_\L)$ contains all refinable faces in the leaf star of $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$. Dividing by the cumulative area $a_\coarseIdxone$ of the faces rescales the integrated face estimator to a density per vertex. This rescaling makes the vertex class residual roughly level- and valence-agnostic. Otherwise, the contribution of coarse faces would be weighted higher and their adjacent vertices would be over-represented during the Dörfler marking step.

\begin{figure}%[htbp]
    \centering
    \begin{subfigure}[b]{0.3\textwidth}
        \centering
        \includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{images/pipeline/pipeline_fig1a.pdf}
        \caption{Coarse domain sequence coloured by the per-face residual $\eta^2_{\l,\coarseIdxone}$ from Equation~\eqref{eq:project_estimator_on_L_to_partition}. }
        \label{fig:pipeline_marking_a}
    \end{subfigure}\hfill
    \begin{subfigure}[b]{0.3\textwidth}
        \centering
        \includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{images/pipeline/pipeline_fig1b.pdf}
        \caption{Per-vertex error residual $\nu^2_\coarseIdxone$ after applying Equation~\eqref{eq:vertex_residual}}
        \label{fig:pipeline_marking_b}
    \end{subfigure}\hfill
\begin{subfigure}[b]{0.3\textwidth}
        \centering
        \includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{images/pipeline/pipeline_fig1c.pdf}
        \caption{Domain sequence after refining the leaf stars of marked vertices to their target levels.}
        \label{fig:pipeline_marking_c}
    \end{subfigure}
    \caption{Visualisations of the individual stages of the \textsc{Mark} and \textsc{Refine} steps.}
    \label{fig:pipeline_marking}
\end{figure}

We rank the vertex classes $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])$ in decreasing order by their residual $\vertClassResidual{\coarseIdxone}$, see Equation~\eqref{eq:vertex_residual}, and enumerate them so that $\vertClassResidual{\coarseIdxone_1} \geq \vertClassResidual{\coarseIdxone_2} \geq \ldots \geq \vertClassResidual{\coarseIdxone_{\vert \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L]) \vert}}$. Dörfler marking~\citep{Doerfler.1996} selects the set $\markedVertexClasses = \{ \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone_1}, \, \dots, \, \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone_m} \}$ for the smallest $m$ such that the face set
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:marked_refinable_residual}
    \B_\theta \; = \bigcup\limits_{p=1}^m \FVleaf{<\L}(\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone_p})
\end{equation}
is a bulk set in the sense of~\eqref{eq:doerfler_selection}. The vertices are ranked by the density $\vertClassResidual{\coarseIdxone}$, but what is accumulated against the bulk criterion is the raw residual $\eta^2_{\l, \fineIdxone}$ of the union of the faces in their leaf stars. As a result, a vertex class whose leaf star is already covered by earlier picks contributes nothing to $\rho_{\B_\theta}$ in the bulk criterion~\eqref{eq:doerfler_selection}.

Note that $\B_\theta$ is the set whose residual is counted, not the set that is refined, and for this variant the latter is strictly larger. Remark~\ref{rem:refinement_atoms} at the end of this section says what that difference does and does not cost, once the operator it depends on is on the page.

\smallskip

The \textsc{Refine} step homogenises the leaf stars of every selected vertex class $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \markedVertexClasses$ using the graded quadrisection operator as in Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline_marking_c}. As a result, it refines the faces of $\B_\theta$ from Equation~\eqref{eq:marked_refinable_residual} and, by the cascade of Remark~\ref{rem:refinement_atoms}, the faces those produce on the way to the target level. We denote by $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ and similarly $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ the finest level and coarsest levels of faces occurring in the leaf star of $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$, cf. Definition~\ref{def:level_range}. The target level map $\targetlevel$ handed to the graded vertex quadrisection operator $\quadriV$ of Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} is given by $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone = \min(\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone} \! + \! 1, \, \L)$.  

The result of the vertex star variant of the \textsc{Refine} step is the domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}$ given by
\begin{equation}
    \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}} = \quadriV \big( \domseq[\L], \, \markedVertexClasses, \, \targetlevel \big).
\end{equation}

The target levels $\targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$ and coarsest levels $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ for all marked vertex classes must be computed on the fixed input domain sequence $\domseq[\L]$, before any refinement takes place. Reading the predicates off the input is what maintains the bounds in Corollary~\ref{cor:graded_quadrisection_leafstar}. It also avoids a cascade of ever increasing target levels. This ensures that the homogenisation $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}$ remains the minimal one in the sense of Lemma~\ref{lemma:graded_quadrisection_minimal}.

\begin{remark}[How the two atoms attack the residual]
\label{rem:refinement_atoms}
    Both the face-based refinement in Section~\ref{subsubsec:refine_face} and the vertex class-based refinement in Section~\ref{subsubsec:refine_vertex} build their bulk set by the same criterion~\eqref{eq:doerfler_selection} and both attack the same residual mass. They differ in how deeply they subdivide it, and that is where their efficiency in degrees of freedom parts.

    The bulk set $\B_\theta$ is the set whose residual is counted, not the set that is refined. For the face variant the two coincide, since $\quadriF$ quadrisects each marked face exactly once. For the vertex variant they do not, because the homogenisation cascades through every level below its target and so also refines faces that only become leaf faces partway through the cascade, and that were never part of any leaf star in~\eqref{eq:marked_refinable_residual}.

    That difference costs nothing in the bulk criterion. Every face the cascade touches is a descendant of a face of $\B_\theta$, and the indicator~\eqref{eq:project_estimator_on_L_to_partition} is additive under quadrisection, since the four children of a face partition its level-$\L$ descendants. Refining a face therefore splits its indicator among its children without creating or destroying any contributions, so the residual mass both variants attack is exactly $\rho_{\B_\theta}$, and~\eqref{eq:doerfler_selection} is the same guarantee in both cases.

    What differs is the depth. The face variant takes each counted face one level. The homogenisation carries it to the target level, which for a leaf star spanning several levels means several quadrisections of its coarse faces. Thus, the resulting space is strictly richer on the same faces. Those coarse faces belong to $\B_\theta$ only because they neighbour a vertex class of large residual. Their own indicator may be small. The extra levels then place degrees of freedom where the indicator did not ask for them. Whether the richer space repays them is a question the bulk criterion cannot answer. Section~\ref{subsubsec:cl_performance} discusses this phenomenon and the costs it can add using numerical results.
\end{remark}
\FloatBarrier

\subsection{Padding substeps}
\label{subsec:padding}

The \textsc{Refine} step in the previous section produces the domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}$. Crucially, $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}$ is not an admissible domain sequence because $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}$ might now contain confined faces, pinch vertices, or boundary pinch vertices. These are the failures of clauses~$(i)$,~$(ii)$ and~$(iii)$ of Definition~\ref{def:subdiv_partition} respectively, so the confinement padding of Section~\ref{subsubsec:confined_faces} discharges the first and the pinch resolution of Section~\ref{subsubsec:pinch_resolution} the other two. The \textsc{Pad} step described in this section repairs these defects and turns $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}$ into an admissible domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pad}}$ while keeping the padded faces added during this process to a minimum to avoid inflating the number of DoFs in non-marked regions. The \textsc{Pad} step is also agnostic to whether the \textsc{Refine} step uses face-based or vertex-star based refinement.

The \textsc{Pad} step has three substeps, run in the order pinch resolution, confinement padding, and grading. We call one application of all three a \emph{padding cycle}. The grading step described in Section~\ref{subsubsec:grading} is optional from an admissibility perspective but helps to transport the error of the adaptive solution across the scales so that it can be resolved, see Section~\ref{subsec:vector_laplace}.

The other two substeps, pinch resolution and confinement padding, aim to restore the admissibility of the refined domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}$. The complexity of the padding algorithm introduced in this section is due to the fact that repairing either of these defects can create new ones. Figure~\ref{fig:padding_interactions_a} illustrates this cross-dependence. Pinch resolution refines a class star, which can confine a leaf face beside it and can create a pinch at another vertex. Confinement padding refines a leaf face, which can confine one of its own children and create a pinch. So all four branches of Figure~\ref{fig:padding_interactions_a} are realised, and every padding substep hands work to the next substep after it. Figure~\ref{fig:candidate_sets} works one instance of this through on a single domain sequence, with the set that each substep hands the other drawn on the mesh it is read from.

The \textsc{Pad} step iterates its substeps until a fixed point is reached at which none of the previous repairs trigger new defect. The architecture of these iterations is visualised in Figure~\ref{fig:padding_interactions_b}. We use an inner loop for pinch resolution, in which only pinch resolution is repeated until we are guaranteed to have a pinch-free domain sequence. Confinement padding is also iterated in an inner loop. Once one inner loop for a given defect terminates, the outer loop moves on to another iteration of the inner loop of the other defect. To control the number of operations needed for these computations, we track candidate sets of vertices and faces that could carry defects after a given repair step between them. The remainder of the section introduces these \emph{candidate sets}.

The grading substep of Section~\ref{subsubsec:grading} tests a third predicate, the level span of Definition~\ref{def:level_range}. It fixes a maximum level span $g \in \mathbb{N}$ and catches the vertex classes that exceed it to obtain
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:grade_set}
    \gradeClasses(\domseq[\L]) \; \coloneq \; \big\{ \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L]) \; \colon \; \levelSpan{\coarseIdxone} > g \big\} .
\end{equation}
Unlike the two sets above this one is not a failure of admissibility. No clause of Definition~\ref{def:subdiv_partition} mentions the level span, and Section~\ref{subsubsec:grading} says why we repair it all the same.

%  with boxes added. Divider line follows fig:refine_interface.
% ============================================================

    % box in y, which is what keeps the two subcaptions on one line.

        \begin{tikzpicture}[
            dbox/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum width=2.5cm, minimum height=0.84cm,
                         align=center, inner sep=3pt, font=\small},

            hdr/.style={font=\small}
        ]
        % The stem carries the repair and the fork carries its two possible
        % effects, so nothing on the picture can be read as a choice.
        \def\xL{0}      \def\xJ{2.7}    \def\xM{5.6}
        \def\yP{1.45}   \def\yC{-1.45}
        \path (-1.4, -2.5) rectangle (7.0, 2.9);   % shared bounding box
        \node[hdr] at (\xL, 2.75) {$\domseq[\L]$};
        \node[hdr] at (\xM, 2.75) {$\domseq[\L]'$};
        \node[dbox] (pL) at (\xL, \yP) {pinch vertex};
        \node[dbox] (cL) at (\xL, \yC) {confined face};
        \node[dbox] (pR) at (\xM, \yP) {pinch vertex};
        \node[dbox] (cR) at (\xM, \yC) {confined face};
        \node[dot] (jP) at (\xJ, \yP) {};
        \node[dot] (jC) at (\xJ, \yC) {};
        \draw[stem] (pL) -- node[lbl, below=3pt, pos=0.5] {pinch\\resolution} (jP);
        \draw[stem] (cL) -- node[lbl, above=3pt, pos=0.5] {confinement\\padding} (jC);
        \draw[arr] (jP) -- (pR);   \draw[arr] (jP) -- (cR);
        \draw[arr] (jC) -- (pR);   \draw[arr] (jC) -- (cR);
        % 2026-09-02: was "collected by \collectV / \collectF". The figure now comes
        % before def:candidate_collection, so it says what has to be re-tested rather
        % than naming the map. rem:padding_interactions attaches the maps later.
        \node[lbl, anchor=south] at (\xM, \yP+0.50) {vertices to re-test};
        \node[lbl, anchor=north] at (\xM, \yC-0.50) {leaf faces to re-test};
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{One repair, two effects.}
        \label{fig:padding_interactions_a}
    \end{subfigure}
    \hfill
    \begin{subfigure}[b]{0.38\textwidth}
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[
            sbox/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum width=2.2cm, minimum height=1.1cm,
                         align=center, inner sep=3pt, font=\small},
            arr/.style={->, >=Stealth, line width=1.0pt, shorten >=2pt, shorten <=1pt},
            cnt/.style={font=\footnotesize, inner sep=1.5pt, align=center},

        ]
        \def\xR{0}      \def\yP{1.45}   \def\yC{-1.45}
        \path (-3.8, -2.5) rectangle (1.5, 2.9);   % shared bounding box
        \node[sbox] (p2) at (\xR, \yP) {pinch\\resolution};
        \node[sbox] (c2) at (\xR, \yC) {confinement\\padding};
        \path[arr] (p2) edge[selfloop] node[cnt, anchor=east, xshift=-3pt] {$q^{\mathrm{pinch}}$\\times} (p2);
        \path[arr] (c2) edge[selfloop] node[cnt, anchor=east, xshift=-3pt] {$q^{\mathrm{confined}}$\\times} (c2);
        \draw[arr] (p2) to[bend left=52] (c2);
        \draw[arr] (c2) to[bend left=52] (p2);

        \draw[inner] (\xR-3.58, \yP-0.88) rectangle (\xR+1.25, \yP+0.88);

    \caption{Cross-dependence of the padding substeps. Panel~\subref{fig:padding_interactions_a} shows both kinds of defects and that any repair of them can create both new pinches and confined faces. All branches are present in general, and each of them leaves behind a set of candidate simplices that the next substep has to re-test. Definition~\ref{def:candidate_collection} below turns those two sets into maps. Panel~\subref{fig:padding_interactions_b} shows the strategy we chose to repair the defects. We isolate repeated applications of pinch resolution and confinement padding into individual inner loops and couple them using an outer loop once they have exhausted their own candidates. Every loop carries the number of times it is traversed.}
    \label{fig:padding_interactions}
\end{figure}

\smallskip

\subsubsection{Candidates for the padding substeps}
\label{subsubsec:locality_candidates}

Every padding substep tests a set of candidates rather than the whole domain sequence and reads the candidates for the next substep off the faces it has refined. The candidate sets track the refinement frontier as it moves through the loop. The candidates sets carry the termination arguments of Section~\ref{subsec:termination} by exhaustion, and they tell the implementation which predicates need to be evaluated on a given cycle.

Section~\ref{subsec:refinement_locality} laid the geometric foundation. A refinement can change a predicate only at the changed states and the new objects of its three splits. We already know from there that new objects are vacuous for vertices. What is left here is to collect those parts into one map per predicate and to ensure that the maps miss nothing.

The following definition collects, for each of the three predicates, the objects that the changed $(\mathrm{C})$ and the new $(\mathrm{N})$ parts can reach. The changed states contribute the objects whose predicates the refinement may have changed, which Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality} places at $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$ and Lemma~\ref{lemma:leafstar_locality} places at the leaf stars that carry it. The new objects contribute what the refinement added, so they cover new predicates to evaluate for the first time. Only the vertex map is spared the second term, by clause $(i)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:new_objects}.

\begin{definition}[Candidate collection]
    \label{def:candidate_collection}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$ with regions $\M_\l$ and let $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ with slices $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$ be a marked family for $\domseq[\L]$ in the sense of~\eqref{eq:marked_face_family}. The map
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:candidate_collection}
        \collectV \big( \domseq[\L], \, \simplexsubsetTwo{} \big) \; \coloneq \bigsqcup\limits_{\l = \lzero}^{\L-1} \; \V_\l \big( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} \big)
    \end{equation}
    collects the vertices of the faces that $\quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$ refines for the first time. Further, $\collectF \big( \domseq[\L], \, \simplexsubsetTwo{} \big) \coloneq \collectF{}^{,c} \, \cup \, \collectF{}^{,n}$ collects the neighbour faces and the children of the faces it refines for the first time given by
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:candidate_collection_faces}
        \collectF{}^{,c} \coloneq \bigsqcup\limits_{\l = \lzero}^{\L-1} \FVl{\l} \big( \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ) \big), \qquad \collectF{}^{,n} \coloneq \bigsqcup\limits_{\l = \lzero}^{\L-1} \Aop{}{\l}{\l+1} \big( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} \big).
    \end{equation}
    Finally, writing $\domseq[\L]' \coloneq \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$ for the refinement, the map $\collectC \big( \domseq[\L], \, \simplexsubsetTwo{} \big) \coloneq \collectC{}^{,n} \, \cup \, \collectC{}^{,c}$ collects the vertex classes it creates and the ones whose level span it can open, given by
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:class_candidate_terms}
        \collectC{}^{,c} \coloneq \big\{ \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L]) \; \colon \;\; \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \;\text{ violates}~\eqref{eq:span_stays} \big\}, \quad \collectC{}^{,n} \coloneq \vertexClasses \big( \domseq[\L]' \big) \setminus \vertexClasses \big( \domseq[\L] \big) .
    \end{equation}
\end{definition}

Each map collects the vertices, faces and vertex classes whose predicates might have changed after applying a quadrisection operator. The first superscript names what is collected: $\bullet$ for vertices, $\triangle$ for faces and $s$ for vertex classes. A second superscript appears wherever a map splits into two terms to name the individual contributions. The maps are indexed by the predicate and not by the substep. What a padding substep receives depends on the defect it tests for and not on the repair that produced the set.

Each map is one row of the grid at the end of Section~\ref{subsec:refinement_locality} with the untouched column dropped, unioned over the levels. The changed part of a split gives the term marked $c$ and the new part gives the term marked $n$. The vertex map carries no $n$ term, because clause $(i)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:new_objects} empties the new vertices. The face map keeps both, since clause $(ii)$ of that lemma bounds a new face without ruling it out. The class map keeps both as well. Its predicate is the only one not read off a refinement fan, so its changed term is governed by Lemma~\ref{lemma:leafstar_locality} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:span_opens} rather than Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality}.

The two changed state terms $\collectF{}^{,c}$ and $\collectC{}^{,c}$ in Definition~\ref{def:candidate_collection} empty out in the special case of marking only confined faces, see Lemma~\ref{lemma:confined_children} and Corollary~\ref{cor:confined_class_candidates}. Those two belong to the substep that supplies the hypothesis and are proved in Section~\ref{subsubsec:confined_faces}.

\smallskip

For the two refinement fan predicates, the vertex map and the face map can now be read as statements about potential defects and not only about candidates, which is the form the exhaustiveness proofs of Section~\ref{subsec:outer_loop} use.

\begin{corollary}[A refinement opens a pinch only at a collected vertex]
    \label{cor:new_pinch_candidates}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$, let $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ be a marked family for $\domseq[\L]$ and let $\domseq[\L]' \coloneq \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$. Write $\V^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ for the (boundary) pinch vertices of a domain sequence, as in~\eqref{eq:pinch_set} of the next section. Then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:new_pinch_candidates}
        \V^{\mathrm{pinch}} \big( \domseq[\L]' \big) \; \setminus \; \V^{\mathrm{pinch}} \big( \domseq[\L] \big) \; \subseteq \; \collectV \big( \domseq[\L], \, \simplexsubsetTwo{} \big) ,
    \end{equation}
    so every pinch the refinement opens is a candidate.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
    Read the level-$\l$ part of the left side against~\eqref{eq:vertex_decomposition_output}. An untouched vertex $(\mathrm{U})$ keeps its refinement fan by Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality} and hence its predicate, so it was already a pinch of $\domseq[\L]$ and the difference removes it. A new vertex $(\mathrm{N})$ is no pinch of $\domseq[\L]'$ at all by clause $(i)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:new_objects}. What is left is the changed vertices $(\mathrm{C})$, which are the level-$\l$ term of~\eqref{eq:candidate_collection} read across the marked family.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[A refinement confines a face only at a collected face]
    \label{cor:new_confined_candidates}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$, let $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ be a marked family for $\domseq[\L]$ and let $\domseq[\L]' \coloneq \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$. Write $\F^{\mathrm{confined}}$ for the confined leaf faces of a domain sequence, as in~\eqref{eq:confined_set} of the next section. Then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:new_confined_candidates}
        \F^{\mathrm{confined}} \big( \domseq[\L]' \big) \; \setminus \; \F^{\mathrm{confined}} \big( \domseq[\L] \big) \; \subseteq \; \collectF \big( \domseq[\L], \, \simplexsubsetTwo{} \big) ,
    \end{equation}
    so every face the refinement confines is a candidate. Lemma~\ref{lemma:confined_children} sharpens this for a marking by confined faces, where the one-ring term $\collectF{}^{,c}$ may be dropped.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
    A confined face of $\domseq[\L]'$ on level $\l$ is a leaf face of $\domseq[\L]'$ on that level by~\eqref{eq:confined_set}, so read the level-$\l$ part of the left side against~\eqref{eq:leaf_face_decomposition_output}. An untouched face $(\mathrm{U})$ is a leaf face of both sequences and shares no vertex with $\simplexsubsetNew{\l}$, so clause $(ii)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality} keeps its confinement reading. It was then already confined in $\domseq[\L]$ and the difference removes it. A changed face $(\mathrm{C})$ lies in the one-ring $\FVl{\l} ( \V_\l ( \simplexsubsetNew{\l} ) )$, which is the level-$\l$ term of $\collectF{}^{,c}$. A new face $(\mathrm{N})$ is a child of a face of $\simplexsubsetNew{\l-1}$ and lies in the level-$(\l \! - \! 1)$ term of $\collectF{}^{,n}$. The three parts exhaust $\leafF{\l}(\domseq[\L]')$, so nothing else can be confined.
\end{proof}

The class map admits the same exhaustiveness. Its statement is the class-side counterpart of the two corollaries above. Its two narrowings belong to the substeps that supply them, so they are proved with those substeps in Sections~\ref{subsubsec:confined_faces} and~\ref{subsubsec:grading}.

\begin{corollary}[A refinement opens a level span only at a collected class]
    \label{cor:span_candidates}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$, let $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ be a marked family for $\domseq[\L]$ and let $\domseq[\L]' \coloneq \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$. With $\gradeClasses$ the classes of level span above $g$ as in~\eqref{eq:grade_set},
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:span_candidates}
        \gradeClasses \big( \domseq[\L]' \big) \; \setminus \; \gradeClasses \big( \domseq[\L] \big) \; \subseteq \; \collectC \big( \domseq[\L], \, \simplexsubsetTwo{} \big) ,
    \end{equation}
    so every level span the refinement opens past $g$ is a candidate.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
    Let $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \gradeClasses(\domseq[\L]')$ with $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \notin \gradeClasses(\domseq[\L])$. An untouched class $(\mathrm{U})$ of~\eqref{eq:vertex_class_decomposition_output} keeps its leaf star by Lemma~\ref{lemma:leafstar_locality} and with it its level span, so the difference on the left carries none. The two remaining cases are the changed and the new classes. If the refinement created the class it lies in $\collectC{}^{,n}$, the new objects, and there is nothing to show. Otherwise $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])$ carries $\levelSpan{\coarseIdxone} \leq g$ and $\levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}' > g$, so its level span grew. Clause $(i)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:span_opens} gives $\levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}' \leq \levelSpan{\coarseIdxone}$ whenever~\eqref{eq:span_stays} holds at the class, so~\eqref{eq:span_stays} fails there and~\eqref{eq:class_candidate_terms} puts $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone}$ into $\collectC{}^{,c}$, the changed states.
\end{proof}

The vertex map and the face map carry all four branches of Figure~\ref{fig:padding_interactions_a} because each of the two admissibility substeps needs a set of candidates to test. The class map has no branch there, since Figure~\ref{fig:padding_interactions_a} draws the two admissibility predicates alone and the level span joins them only in Figure~\ref{fig:padding_signal_flow} in Section~\ref{subsec:padding_loop}.

The pinch resolution and the grading are the two substeps that repair with $\quadriV$, and both meet the hypothesis of clause $(iii)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:span_opens}, which supplies the bounds on the coarsest and finest levels of the leaf stars of the refined vertex classes. The grading targets $\lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone} \! - \! g$ by~\eqref{eq:grading_detection_target}. The pinch resolution targets $\l \! + \! 1$ for the finest pinch level $\l$ of the class by~\eqref{eq:pinch_detection_target}, which the second case of~\eqref{eq:class_star_levels} confines to the levels $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone} \leq \l < \lmaxIdx{\coarseIdxone}$.

The two substeps that restore admissibility now follow, one section each, in the order the cycle runs them. Both have the shape set out above. A candidate set is tested on a fixed input, everything that fires is repaired at once, and Definition~\ref{def:candidate_collection} reads the candidates of the next cycle off what the repair refined. A candidate set is a superset of the defects and the detection filters it, so a non-empty candidate set is no claim that a defect appeared. Both are iterated to a fixed point of their own predicate before the next substep begins, which is the arrangement Figure~\ref{fig:padding_interactions_b} boxes, and each carries its own bound on the number of cycles it needs. The grading follows them in Section~\ref{subsubsec:grading}, and Section~\ref{subsec:outer_loop} closes the outer loop around all three.

\subsubsection{Resolving vertex pinches}
\label{subsubsec:pinch_resolution}

This substep is the inner pinch resolution loop of Figure~\ref{fig:padding_interactions_b}. It detects pinches with Definitions~\ref{def:pinch_vertex} and~\ref{def:boundary_pinch} and repairs them with the graded vertex quadrisection operator $\quadriV$. The pinch vertices $\V^{\mathrm{pinch}}(\domseq[\L])$ of~\eqref{eq:pinch_set} are the defects this substep repairs. The pinch detection defined below derives the arguments of the graded vertex quadrisection $\quadriV$ of Definition~\ref{def:graded_vertex_quadrisection} from the pinch vertices $\V^{\mathrm{pinch}}$, namely the vertex classes that contain pinches and a target level that repairs the finest pinch of each vertex class, relative to a candidate set.

\begin{definition}[Pinch detection]
    \label{def:pinch_detection}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$ and let $\V^{\mathrm{cand}} \subseteq \V(\domseq[\L])$ be a set of candidate vertices, each read at a level. \emph{Pinch detection} is the map
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:pinch_detection}
        \detectV \big( \domseq[\L], \, \V^{\mathrm{cand}} \big) \; \coloneq \; \big( \pinchClasses, \, \targetlevel \big)
    \end{equation}
    into the vertex classes caught among the candidates
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:pinch_detection_classes}
        \pinchClasses \; \coloneq \; \big\{ \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \vertexClasses(\domseq[\L]) \; \colon \;\; \vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \in \V^{\mathrm{cand}} \;\text{ is a pinch of }\; \domseq[\L] \;\text{ at least on one level }\; \l \big\},
    \end{equation}
    and their target level map
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:pinch_detection_target}
        \targetlevel \; \colon \;\; \pinchClasses \; \to \; \{ \lzero, \, \dots, \, \L \}, \qquad \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \; \mapsto \; \max \big\{ \, \l \! + \! 1 \; \colon \;\; \vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \in \V^{\mathrm{pinch}}(\domseq[\L]) \big\} .
    \end{equation}
    The set on the right of~\eqref{eq:pinch_detection_target} is non-empty for every $\vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \pinchClasses$ by~\eqref{eq:pinch_detection_classes}, so the maximum exists. A vertex has an empty refinement fan at level $\L$, so no pinches exist on level $\L$ and the maximum never exceeds $\L$.
\end{definition}

Applying the vertex quadrisection operator once homogenises every detected pinch class in $\pinchClasses$ to its target $\targetlevel$ in the sense of~\eqref{eq:homogenisation_constraint}. A single application resolves the detected pinches. It does not leave the domain sequence free of pinches, since the repair can open a new pinch beside the ones it closes. Corollary~\ref{cor:new_pinch_candidates} places every such pinch in $\collectV$, so the substep iterates over that candidate set until it is exhausted and the domain sequence is pinch-free.

\begin{iteration}[Pinch resolution]
\label{it:pinch_iteration}
    The pinch resolution substep is initialised with the domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},0} \coloneq \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}$ and candidate set $\V^{\mathrm{cand},0} \coloneq \V^{\mathrm{seed}}$, where $\V^{\mathrm{seed}} \subseteq \V(\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}})$ is a given initial candidate set. It then iterates the following \emph{pinch resolution cycle} for $t \in \{1, \, \ldots, \, q^{\mathrm{pinch}} \}$:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=3pt, itemsep=-2pt]
    \item Detect pinches for the candidate set $\V^{\mathrm{cand},t-1}$ of the previous cycle on the domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t-1}$ to obtain
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:pinch_round_detection}
        \big( \pinchClasses[t], \, \targetlevel^t \big) \; \coloneq \; \detectV \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t-1}, \, \V^{\mathrm{cand},t-1} \big).
    \end{equation}
    \vspace{-7mm}
    \item Repair the detected pinches by homogenising every caught class to its target
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:pinch_round_repair}
        \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t} \; \coloneq \; \quadriV \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t-1}, \, \pinchClasses[t], \, \targetlevel^t \big).
    \end{equation}
    \vspace{-7mm}
    \item Collect the candidates for the next cycle based on the marked family $\simplexsubsetTwo[t]{}$ that~\eqref{eq:graded_marked_family} builds from $\pinchClasses[t]$ and $\targetlevel^t$, less what Corollary~\ref{cor:pinch_spent} has already spent,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:pinch_candidates}
        \V^{\mathrm{cand},t} \coloneq \collectV \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t-1}, \, \simplexsubsetTwo[t]{} \big) \; \setminus \; \big\{ \vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \; \colon \; \vertexClass{\coarseIdxone} \in \pinchClasses[t] \; \text{ and } \; \l < \targetlevel^t_\coarseIdxone \big\}.
    \end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
We repeat steps $1$ to $3$ until the pinch detection in~\eqref{eq:pinch_round_detection} detects no more pinches. We then set $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch}} \coloneq \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t}$ and write $q^{\mathrm{pinch}} \coloneq t \! - \! 1$ for the number of cycles until the fixed point was reached.

Finally, the candidate sets for the other two substeps are collected. We read them off the productive cycles once the fixed point is reached,
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:pinch_handover}
    \F^{\mathrm{pinch}} \; \coloneq \bigcup\limits_{t = 1}^{q^{\mathrm{pinch}}} \collectF \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t-1}, \, \simplexsubsetTwo[t]{} \big)
    \qquad \text{and} \qquad
    \vertexClasses^{\mathrm{pinch}} \; \coloneq \bigcup\limits_{t = 1}^{q^{\mathrm{pinch}}} \collectC \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t-1}, \, \simplexsubsetTwo[t]{} \big) ,
\end{equation}
both read on the marked family of the cycle as Definition~\ref{def:candidate_collection} asks. The stopping cycle contributes nothing to either union, since it marks no class. The substep returns $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ together with the two sets of~\eqref{eq:pinch_handover}.
\end{iteration}

The iteration stops when the pinch detection catches nothing, i.e. $\pinchClasses[t] = \varnothing$. That is an empty marked family $\simplexsubsetTwo[t]{}$, so~\eqref{eq:graded_vertex_quadrisection} adjoins nothing and~\eqref{eq:pinch_candidates} leaves $\V^{\mathrm{cand},t} = \varnothing$. It is also the fixed point $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t} = \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t-1}$ of the iteration.

The first column of Figure~\ref{fig:candidate_sets} shows one pinch resolution loop with two cycles. Its first cycle repairs the pinch of the input and opens another one beside it, which is what the second cycle then has to repair.

The seed $\V^{\mathrm{seed}}$ may always be taken to be all vertices $\V(\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}})$ of the input domain sequence. A smaller seed can be used whenever the domain sequence entering the substep is already known to be free of pinches outside a given vertex set, which is the case in every pass of the overarching padding loop in Section~\ref{subsec:outer_loop}.

\begin{corollary}[The pinch iteration terminates]
    \label{cor:pinch_rounds}
    The number $q^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ of productive pinch resolution cycles of Iteration~\ref{it:pinch_iteration} above is bounded by the room for refinement the input domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}$ with regions $\M^{\mathrm{refine}}_\l$ leaves inside the hierarchy, i.e.
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:pinch_rounds}
        q^{\mathrm{pinch}} \; \leq \; \sum\limits_{\l = 1}^{\L} \; \big\vert \, \F_\l(\T_\l) \setminus \M^{\mathrm{refine}}_\l \, \big\vert \, .
    \end{equation}
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
    Every productive cycle marks at least one vertex class, and every marked class has $\lminIdx{\coarseIdxone} < \targetlevel_\coarseIdxone$, since a pinch on level $\l$ carries the target $\l \! + \! 1$. The repair is therefore not the identity by Corollary~\ref{cor:graded_quadrisection_composition} and thus $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ contains at least one face. Regions only grow, and every $\M^{\mathrm{refine}}_\l$ is bounded by $\F_\l(\T_\l)$. The sum starts at $\l = 1$ since $\F_\lzero(\T_{\lzero}) = \M_\lzero^{\mathrm{refine}}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The pinch iteration discharges clauses (ii) and (iii)]
    \label{prop:pinch_discharged}
    Let the seed cover the pinches of the input domain sequence, i.e. $\V^{\mathrm{pinch}}(\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{refine}}) \subseteq \V^{\mathrm{seed}}$. Then no vertex of the output $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ of Iteration~\ref{it:pinch_iteration} is a pinch or a boundary pinch on any level, so $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ satisfies clauses~(ii) and~(iii) of Definition~\ref{def:subdiv_partition}.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
    The iteration reaches its stopping cycle by Corollary~\ref{cor:pinch_rounds}, so the output $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ exists, and the stopping rule says that the detection~\eqref{eq:pinch_round_detection} catches nothing there. It remains to see that the detection tests enough, that is $\V^{\mathrm{pinch}}(\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t}) \subseteq \V^{\mathrm{cand},t}$ for every cycle $t$. We show this by induction, the case $t = 0$ being the hypothesis on the seed. Consider $\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \in \V^{\mathrm{pinch}}(\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t})$. It cannot have been a pinch of $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t-1}$, because it would then lie in $\V^{\mathrm{cand},t-1}$ by induction, hence be caught with $\targetlevel^t_\coarseIdxone > \l$, and Corollary~\ref{cor:pinch_spent} forbids a pinch below the target. So the pinch is new, and Corollary~\ref{cor:new_pinch_candidates} puts $\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone}$ into the candidate set $\collectV(\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t-1}, \simplexsubsetTwo[t]{})$. The set subtracted in~\eqref{eq:pinch_candidates} carries no pinch of $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch},t}$, again by Corollary~\ref{cor:pinch_spent}, so $\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone} \in \V^{\mathrm{cand},t}$. Thus, an empty detection on the stopping cycle implies the absence of pinch vertices, i.e. $\V^{\mathrm{pinch}}(\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch}}) = \varnothing$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why the bound is not local]
    \label{rem:pinch_rounds_bound}
    The right-hand side of~\eqref{eq:pinch_rounds} is the refinement room the input leaves in the hierarchy. The corollary therefore settles only eventual termination, and better upper bounds remain an open question in this work. This remark explains where a sharper bound would have to come from and why it is difficult to achieve.

    The iteration is local from one cycle to the next, since~\eqref{eq:candidate_collection} advances the candidates only to the vertices of the faces the repair refined for the first time.
    A sharper bound would factor the cycle count $q^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ into two contributions. The first is the number of classes ever reached by the expanding frontier on a given level. The second is the number of times a single class can be caught within the frontiers across all levels.

    The second factor is bounded by the level range of the class alone. A class caught at level $\l$ is repaired to target $\l \! + \! 1$, and Corollary~\ref{cor:pinch_spent} then forbids it to be a pinch on any level below that target. Every later catch of the same class is at a strictly higher level, so the class is caught at most $\L - \lminIdx{\coarseIdxone}$ times.

    The first factor is what we cannot currently bound. Every iteration advances the frontier and can create another pinch in turn. Figure~\ref{fig:padding_chain} shows the mechanism and Section~\ref{subsubsec:pass_count} discusses it, where stricter versions of this chain drive the count of outer padding cycles $q$. Nothing in the argument limits how far a chain runs, so the recruitment stops only when it runs out of mesh, which is what~\eqref{eq:pinch_rounds} reports.

    Problem~\ref{prob:padding_passes} asks for a bound on the outer count $q$ in terms of the control mesh and for a localisation of the faces $\textsc{Pad}$ refines. The inner count $q^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ has the same status and is open for the same reason.

\robert{The chains described in the previous paragraph are short in practice on the meshes we run, even though we do not prove that.} \guidance{This paragraph used to cite Table~\ref{tab:padding_interface_L4} for $q^{\mathrm{pinch}}$. That table reports the outer count $q$ and no inner count, so the citation was wrong and is removed. Re-running the table with a $q^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ and a $q^{\mathrm{confined}}$ column would restore the claim.}
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Resolving confined faces}
\label{subsubsec:confined_faces}

This substep is the confinement padding of Figure~\ref{fig:padding_interactions_b}. It detects confined faces and repairs them with the face quadrisection operator $\quadriF$. The confined leaf faces $\F^{\mathrm{confined}}(\domseq[\L])$ of~\eqref{eq:confined_set} are the defects this substep repairs.
Detection needs no definition of its own here. The detection of confined faces is the evaluation of the predicate of Definition~\ref{def:confined_face} on the candidate set, and such a set of leaf faces is already a marked family in the sense of~\eqref{eq:marked_face_family}. We write $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ for the faces the detection marks, which is the set the repair is applied to. The repair is then one application of the face quadrisection, i.e.
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:confined_faces_resolved_subdiv_partition}
    \quadriF \big( \domseq[\L], \, \simplexsubsetTwo{} \big) \qquad \text{for a set} \qquad \simplexsubsetTwo{} \; \subseteq \; \F^{\mathrm{confined}}(\domseq[\L]) .
\end{equation}

One application does not leave a domain sequence free of confined faces. The children of a confined face on level $\l$ inherit its neighbourhood. Suppose that neighbourhood is refined two or more levels beyond $\l$. Then the child at the corner shared by the finer faces has no refinement-exterior vertex on level $\l \! + \! 1$ either and is confined in turn. The substep is therefore an iteration over a candidate set, in the same shape as the pinch resolution.

\begin{lemma}[Confinement padding confines only its own children]
    \label{lemma:confined_children}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$, let $\simplexsubsetTwo{} \subseteq \F^{\mathrm{confined}}(\domseq[\L])$ with slices $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$, and let $\domseq[\L]' \coloneq \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$ as in~\eqref{eq:confined_faces_resolved_subdiv_partition}. Then every face that is confined in $\domseq[\L]'$ and was not confined in $\domseq[\L]$ is a child of a face of $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$. In the two terms of~\eqref{eq:candidate_collection_faces} this reads
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:confined_children}
        \F^{\mathrm{confined}} \big( \domseq[\L]' \big) \; \setminus \; \F^{\mathrm{confined}} \big( \domseq[\L] \big) \; \subseteq \; \collectF{}^{,n} \big( \domseq[\L], \, \simplexsubsetTwo{} \big) \; = \bigsqcup\limits_{\l = \lzero}^{\L-1} \Aop{}{\l}{\l+1} \big( \simplexsubsetTwo{\l} \big) ,
    \end{equation}
    so the one-ring term $\collectF{}^{,c}$ may be dropped and the children term alone is a sufficient candidate set for the inner loop of the confinement padding substep.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    Every face of $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$ is a leaf face of $\domseq[\L]$ by~\eqref{eq:confined_set} and therefore carries no children, so no slice $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$ meets $\ancestorPatch{\M_{\l+1}}$. Every marked face is refined for the first time, that is $\simplexsubsetNew{\l} = \simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$ in the sense of~\eqref{eq:newly_refined_slice}, which is the equality in~\eqref{eq:confined_children}.

    Corollary~\ref{cor:new_confined_candidates} already places every face the repair confines in $\collectF{} = \collectF{}^{,c} \cup \collectF{}^{,n}$, so only the one-ring term has to be discharged. Its faces are the changed faces $(\mathrm{C})$ of~\eqref{eq:leaf_face_decomposition_output}, the leaf faces of $\domseq[\L]$ that survive and meet a refined vertex. So let $\face{\l}{\fineIdxone}$ be a leaf face of both that is unconfined in $\domseq[\L]$, and let $\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone}$ be one of its vertices that is refinement-exterior on level $\l$. A face of the slice $\simplexsubsetTwo{\l}$ is confined and has no refinement-exterior vertex on level $\l$ by Definition~\ref{def:confined_face}, so $\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone}$ is a vertex of no such face. Lemma~\ref{lemma:pad_locality} therefore leaves $\refFan{\l}(\vertex{\l}{\coarseIdxone})$ unchanged, the vertex stays refinement-exterior and $\face{\l}{\fineIdxone}$ stays unconfined. The inclusion in~\eqref{eq:confined_children} follows.
\end{proof}

The candidate collection of this substep admits a second narrowing, for the classes the grading of Section~\ref{subsubsec:grading} has to test. It is already proved. The faces the repair marks are confined leaf faces by~\eqref{eq:confined_set} and~\eqref{eq:confined_faces_resolved_subdiv_partition}, which is the hypothesis of clause~$(ii)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:span_opens}, so~\eqref{eq:face_span_bounds} holds at every class of the input.

\begin{corollary}[Confinement padding collects no changed class]
    \label{cor:confined_class_candidates}
    Let $\domseq[\L] \in \alldomseqs_\L$ and let $\domseq[\L]' = \quadriF(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{})$ for a set $\simplexsubsetTwo{} \subseteq \F^{\mathrm{confined}}(\domseq[\L])$ of confined faces on $\domseq[\L]$.Then~\eqref{eq:span_stays} holds at every class of $\vertexClasses(\domseq[\L])$ and no level span grows. No changed class is then a candidate, so $\collectC{}^{,c}(\domseq[\L], \simplexsubsetTwo{}) = \varnothing$ and the collection map $\collectC$ of Definition~\ref{def:candidate_collection} simplifies to $\collectC{}^{,n}$, the new classes $(\mathrm{N})$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
    Clause $(ii)$ of Lemma~\ref{lemma:span_opens} says that~\eqref{eq:span_stays} holds at every class of $\domseq[\L]$. No class violates it, which is what $\collectC{}^{,c}$ of~\eqref{eq:class_candidate_terms} collects.
\end{proof}

This substep therefore hands the grading only the classes it creates, which are the midpoints born on the children of $\simplexsubsetTwo{}$. This is the class counterpart of the narrowing above, and the term it drops is the one the face side keeps.

\begin{iteration}[Confinement padding]
\label{it:confined_iteration}
    The confinement padding step is initialised with the domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},0} \coloneq \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ and candidate set $\F^{\mathrm{cand},0} \coloneq \F^{\mathrm{seed}}$, where $\F^{\mathrm{seed}} \subseteq \leafMesh(\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch}})$ is a given initial candidate set. It then iterates the following \emph{confinement cycle} for $t \in \{1, \, \ldots, \, q^{\mathrm{confined}} \}$:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=3pt, itemsep=-2pt]
    \item Detect confined faces for the candidate set $\F^{\mathrm{cand},t-1}$ of the previous cycle on the domain sequence $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t-1}$ to obtain
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:confined_cycle_detection}
        \simplexsubsetTwo[t]{} \; \coloneq \; \big\{ \face{\l}{\fineIdxone} \in \F^{\mathrm{cand},t-1} \; \colon \;\; \face{\l}{\fineIdxone} \;\text{ is confined in }\; \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t-1} \big\} .
    \end{equation}
    \vspace{-7mm}
    \item Repair the detected faces by refining every one of them by one level,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:confined_cycle_repair}
        \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t} \; \coloneq \; \quadriF \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t-1}, \, \simplexsubsetTwo[t]{} \big).
    \end{equation}
    \vspace{-7mm}
    \item Collect the candidates for the next cycle based on the marked faces $\simplexsubsetTwo[t]{}$, less the one-ring term that Lemma~\ref{lemma:confined_children} has dropped,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:confined_candidates}
        \F^{\mathrm{cand},t} \; \coloneq \; \collectF{}^{,n} \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t-1}, \, \simplexsubsetTwo[t]{} \big).
    \end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
We repeat steps $1$ to $3$ until the confinement detection in~\eqref{eq:confined_cycle_detection} detects no more confined faces. We then set $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined}} \coloneq \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t}$ and write $q^{\mathrm{confined}} \coloneq t \! - \! 1$ for the number of cycles until the fixed point was reached.

Finally, the candidate sets for the other two substeps are collected. We read them off the productive cycles once the fixed point is reached,
\begin{align}
    \label{eq:confined_handover_1}
    \V^{\mathrm{confined}} \; &\coloneq \bigcup\limits_{t = 1}^{q^{\mathrm{confined}}} \collectV \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t-1}, \, \simplexsubsetTwo[t]{} \big), \\
    \label{eq:confined_handover_2}
    \vertexClasses^{\mathrm{confined}} \; &\coloneq \bigcup\limits_{t = 1}^{q^{\mathrm{confined}}} \collectC{}^{,n} \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t-1}, \, \simplexsubsetTwo[t]{} \big) .
\end{align}
The stopping cycle contributes nothing to either union, since it marks no face. The substep returns $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined}}$ together with the two sets of~\eqref{eq:confined_handover_1} and~\eqref{eq:confined_handover_2}.
\end{iteration}

The iteration stops when the confinement detection catches nothing, i.e. $\simplexsubsetTwo[t]{} = \varnothing$. That is an empty marked family, so~\eqref{eq:confined_cycle_repair} adds no refinement and~\eqref{eq:confined_candidates} leaves $\F^{\mathrm{cand},t} = \varnothing$. It is also the fixed point $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t} = \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t-1}$ of the iteration.

The second column of Figure~\ref{fig:candidate_sets} shows one confinement padding loop with two cycles. It shows how the loop is seeded from a previous pinch resolution loop, and tracks the successive face candidate sets until they are exhausted.

The seed $\F^{\mathrm{seed}}$ may always be taken to be all leaf faces $\leafMesh(\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch}})$ of the input domain sequence. A smaller seed can be used whenever that sequence is already known to carry no confined face outside a given set, which is the case in every pass of the overarching padding loop in Section~\ref{subsec:outer_loop}.

\begin{corollary}[The confinement iteration advances one level per cycle]
    \label{cor:confinement_cycles}
    Let $\l_t$ denote the coarsest level carrying a face of $\simplexsubsetTwo[t]{}$ at cycle $t$. Then $\l_{t+1} \geq \l_t \! + \! 1$, and the number $q^{\mathrm{confined}}$ of productive cycles of Iteration~\ref{it:confined_iteration} satisfies
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:confinement_cycles}
        q^{\mathrm{confined}} \; \leq \; \L - \l_1 \; \leq \; \L,
    \end{equation}
    where $\l_1$ is the level of the coarsest confined face of the input domain sequence.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
    By Lemma~\ref{lemma:confined_children} the set $\simplexsubsetTwo[t+1]{}$ is contained in the candidate set $\F^{\mathrm{cand},t}$ of~\eqref{eq:confined_candidates}, whose faces are children of faces of $\simplexsubsetTwo[t]{}$ and hence lie on levels $\lone \geq \l_t \! + \! 1$. Equality can fail, because no child of a face on level $\l_t$ has to be confined. A face whose neighbourhood is refined exactly one level beyond $\l_t$ leaves four children with a refinement-exterior vertex on level $\l_t \! + \! 1$ and drops out, and $\l_{t+1}$ is then read off whichever face of $\simplexsubsetTwo[t]{}$ is buried deeper. No leaf face on level $\L$ is ever confined, since every vertex is refinement-exterior on level $\L$ by Definition~\ref{def:refinement_fan}. Hence $\l_t \leq \L \! - \! 1$ for every productive cycle, which gives~\eqref{eq:confinement_cycles}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The confinement iteration discharges clause (i)]
    \label{prop:confined_discharged}
    Let the seed cover the confined faces of the input so that $\F^{\mathrm{confined}}(\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{pinch}}) \subseteq \F^{\mathrm{seed}}$. Then no leaf face of the output $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined}}$ of Iteration~\ref{it:confined_iteration} is confined, so $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined}}$ satisfies clause~(i) of Definition~\ref{def:subdiv_partition}.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
    The iteration reaches its stopping cycle by Corollary~\ref{cor:confinement_cycles}, so the output exists, and the stopping rule says that the detection~\eqref{eq:confined_cycle_detection} catches nothing there. The induction of Proposition~\ref{prop:pinch_discharged} then runs unchanged on the invariant
    \begin{equation*}
        \F^{\mathrm{confined}} \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t} \big) \; \subseteq \; \F^{\mathrm{cand},t} .
    \end{equation*} A face already confined in $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t-1}$ lies in $\F^{\mathrm{cand},t-1}$ by induction, so~\eqref{eq:confined_cycle_repair} refines it and it is no longer a leaf face, which replaces the appeal to Corollary~\ref{cor:pinch_spent}. A newly confined face is a child of $\simplexsubsetTwo[t]{}$ by Lemma~\ref{lemma:confined_children} and hence lies in $\F^{\mathrm{cand},t}$.
\end{proof}

Under the hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:confined_discharged} the detection of every cycle is exhaustive,
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:confined_detection_exhaustive}
    \simplexsubsetTwo[t]{} \; = \; \F^{\mathrm{confined}} \big( \domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t-1} \big) ,
\end{equation}
since the invariant of the proof puts every confined face of $\domseq[\L]^{\mathrm{confined},t-1}$ among the candidates $\F^{\mathrm{cand},t-1}$. Without a covering seed the inclusion can be strict. That is why Iteration~\ref{it:confined_iteration} is stated with $\simplexsubsetTwo[t]{}$ and not with the predicate set itself.

The two cycle counts $q^{\mathrm{pinch}}$ of Corollary~\ref{cor:pinch_rounds} and $q^{\mathrm{confined}}$ of Corollary~\ref{cor:confinement_cycles} are the counts of the two inner iterations. They are not part of the count $q$ of productive $\textsc{Pad}$ passes of Section~\ref{subsec:outer_loop}, which counts the passes driven by the links between the substeps alone, see Figure~\ref{fig:padding_interactions_b}.

Figure~\ref{fig:candidate_sets} runs both inner iterations on one domain sequence. Every cycle panel carries the candidate set that cycle tests and the set it hands the other substep, so the two counts and the two handovers of~\eqref{eq:pinch_handover} and~\eqref{eq:confined_handover_1} can be read off the mesh. Both substep iteration counts are $2$ in the first outer pass, and the pass that follows is unproductive, so the counter of the outer loop from Section~\ref{subsec:padding_loop} is $q = 1$.

    ]

    \draw[arr] (IN.north) -- (IN.north |- P1.west)

    % ---- the debug frame, see \candbox above. Drawn last so it picks up
